  • Chapter 1029: But I Can Understand (4)

It wasn’t particularly overwhelming.

The majority of those occupying Plum Blossom Island were pirates, but they had already lost their momentum. On the other hand, the disciples of Mount Hua and the Tang family, who had dug into them, were pressuring Jang Ilso around Hyun Jong with a stern attitude.

In short, it was Jang Ilso who was surrounded.

Nevertheless, the moment he faced Jang Ilso, Hyun Jong felt as if his body was being swept away by a typhoon.

He had already felt this sensation once during the initiation of the Heavenly Friends Alliance in the past.

But the sensation he felt now was even greater and more intense than before. It was enough to make him wonder if he could face Jang Ilso.

The leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. And the sect leader of Mount Hua.

That name clearly deserved to stand alone with the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance. However, as a warrior, Hyun Jong felt too small to face Jang Ilso.

That was why he remained silent.

When Jang Ilso, who had entered Plum Blossom Island alone and confidently, tried to negotiate with Chung Myung instead of him, Hyun Jong simply remained silent. That was because he felt it was more appropriate.

However... Chung Myung sent Jang Ilso in front of him. As if it was Hyun Jong who had to deal with him.

‘Chung Myung told the kids over and over again.’

Those who hold swords must know the weight of the swords in their hands. They must never forget that what they hold in their hands is a weapon meant to take people’s lives.

That obvious statement struck Hyun Jong once again.

Chung Myung said he was the sword of Mount Hua.

If he was the sword, it must be the sharpest sword in the world. If so, Hyun Jong, who held that sword in his hand, must also know its weight.

That meant...

Hyun Jong glanced at Chung Myung. He was still looking at him with an unwavering gaze.

‘I guess that means I, too, must be qualified enough to hold that sword.’

That was probably not what Chung Myung intended. But at least Hyun Jong felt it that way. That was the burden he had felt all along.

Now, Mount Hua was by no means the same sect as it used to be.

For him, who had acquired the excessive position of Sect Leader of Mount Hua by simply enduring, it was only natural that he wished for something more than the past.

‘Can I handle it, though?’

Such sharp swords?

It wasn’t just Chung Myung. The sword called Baek Cheon, the sword called Yu Yiseol, the sword called Yoon Jong, and Jo Gul... did he really have the ability to properly wield all those swords of Mount Hua?

There was nothing in the world more scary than a blindly wielded sword. To hold a sword that wasn’t suitable for one’s ability was perhaps close to a curse. To him and to the sword in his hand.

Before he could answer all such questions, Jang Ilso opened his mouth.

“Is it okay to call you Alliance Leader? Or...?”

“Sect Leader is enough.”

Hyun Jong drew the line.

There was the Tang family there. The moment the title turned to Alliance Leader, the choice of Hyun Jong would determine the Tang family’s will. That was not what Hyun Jong wanted.

However, at that moment, Jang Ilso smiled.

“Humility is a good thing.”

“...”

“If it isn’t cowardice.”

Hyun Jong closed his eyes tightly.

If the person standing there had been the abbot, he might have felt insulted by those words. If it had been Namgung Hwang, he would have been furious, and even Tang Gunak, who rarely showed any emotions, would have been displaying killing intent.

However, ironically, Hyun Jong didn’t feel any anger towards such words from Jang Ilso. He had been through too much to feel humiliated by Jang Ilso’s words, and the gap between him and Jang Ilso was too big. Rather, that was why he could be calm.

“My capacity is not that great.”

Jang Ilso raised his eyebrows at that calm voice. It seemed as if he had not expected that answer at all.

After staring at Hyun Jong silently for a while, he slowly nodded his head.

“... okay, sect leader.”

There was not a hint of hostility in Jang Ilso’s smiling face.

Some might feel it was a pretense, and others might be wary of the sinisterness hidden in that smile. However, Hyun Jong accepted that smile as it was.

Jang Ilso was a person who wasn’t aware of Hyun Jong’s very existence. There was no need for such a person to show hostility towards Hyun Jong.

It was familiar to Hyun Jong.

Before Chung Myung came in, when he was the sajil to the then-sect leader, those who met him always smiled. Human hostility started with threats. It was also human to be infinitely gentle towards people who were not a threat.

That was why Hyun Jong felt that smile was both appropriate and painful.

“As the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance, I formally request the sect leader of Mount Hua. Please show mercy to the Black Dragon King, a leader in the Four Precepts Alliance who is being subdued by Mount Hua.”

Hyun Jong’s knees shook at the words of Jang Ilso.

He turned his gaze slightly as if trying to find an answer, but Chung Myung held no expression. Chung Myung, who usually tried to convey his intentions through his expression whenever he had a choice, was not there. He just looked at Hyun Jong with an expressionless face that didn’t suit him.

As if he had made a decision and was just carrying it out, he held the sword to the throat of the Black Dragon King.

Hyun Jong took a deep breath and stared at Jang Ilso.

“I will ask you just what my disciple has asked.”

“...”

“Why should we release the Black Dragon King?”

“The reason is quite simple.”

Jang Ilso stared at Hyun Jong with a slightly dark gaze.

“Because that is the only way for Mount Hua, the Tang family, and the Namgung family to escape this island safely.”

A low sigh escaped from Hyun Jong’s mouth.

Actually, if one thought about it, the reason wasn’t wrong.

Clearly, the Black Dragon King has been subdued. However, the pirates’ forces, excluding the Black Dragon King, remain largely intact. If the Black Dragon King were to die, they would first follow Jang Ilso’s orders.

Common sense would suggest there would be confusion during the transfer of command...

‘Since the opponent is Jang Ilso, that kind of thing wouldn’t happen.’

His position as the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance and his reputation as the Hegemony Lord made Jang Ilso a symbol of the Evil Sect. If Jang Ilso, rather than the Black Dragon King, were to command the pirates, morale might actually increase.

Furthermore, it’s unlikely that Jang Ilso didn’t prepare for this situation in advance.

In the end, to escape this island, he would have to confront the remnants of the pirates and the Ten Thousand People clan, who had not yet moved.

Even his foothold has now been lost.

Hyun Jong’s gaze unconsciously turned to the other side of the river.

If Shaolin on the riverside moved, it might be worthwhile to fight...

‘But that is a difficult thing to hope for.’

Hyun Jong had a gut feeling. Shaolin would not move.

If they had intended to join forces, they should have moved together before the situation deteriorated when Mount Hua attacked the island. They would just watch, having missed their chance.

“Are you saying that if we release the Black Dragon King, you will send us back across the Yangtze River?”

“Of course.”

Jang Ilso answered with a soft smile.

There was no need to judge whether that statement was true or not.

It wasn’t because Hyun Jong trusted Jang Ilso. In fact, if you were to choose the person in this world who trusted Jang Ilso the least, it would be Hyun Jong.

But that was why he could trust him.

A big liar didn’t lie about small things. Those who tried to deceive the world were usually truthful.

From Hyun Jong’s perspective, the Black Dragon King’s life was a small matter. However, from Jang Ilso’s perspective, it’s definitely not a trivial matter that would undermine the world’s trust in him.

“What will you do?”

Jang Ilso gently pressured Hyun Jong.

“Would you release the Black Dragon King and go home in peace? Or...”

Jang Ilso’s nails scratched his lips.

“Here.”

The moment his nails left his lips, a chilling voice leaked from Jang Ilso’s mouth.

“Should I kill you all here?”

This was clearly a threat, sending a chill down Hyun Jong’s spine.

Hyun Jong forcefully suppressed his racing heart.

Should he cut off the Black Dragon King’s head and aim for negotiation with Jang Ilso?

Or should he leave Mount Hua, the Tang family, and Namgung alive and retreat like this for their safety?

This was probably a difficult decision that no one in the world could easily make.

But...

Hyun Jong looked at Mount Hua’s disciples. They were all looking at him with eyes full of trust.

No matter what came out of his mouth, they would simply believe and follow, even if the price for that choice was their death; they would gladly accept it.

The one who stood in the position representing Mount Hua must naturally accept such a gaze. To do that, he must become an incredibly great person.

But...

A faint smile formed on Hyun Jong’s lips.

‘I am a small person.’

Acknowledging that fact made him feel better. He was not the kind of person they wanted him to be.

That was why he could understand. He didn’t know what choice the sect leader of Mount Hua should make, but it was clear what choice Hyun Jong had to make.

Hyun Jong looked at Chung Myung one last time.

‘Will you be fine?’

A small smile appeared on Chung Myung’s lips as he understood the true meaning of the gaze. It seemed to say that it was enough.

Hyun Jong finally took a deep breath and looked at Jang Ilso.

No matter how much he had made up his mind, Jang Ilso was still an overwhelming presence.

However, Hyun Jong didn’t necessarily need to be greater than Jang Ilso.

“Right...”

Jang Ilso tilted his head slightly.

“Have you made up your mind, sect leader?”

Hyun Jong opened his mouth calmly, still facing that gentle smile.

“Alliance lord.”

“Go ahead.”

“Mount Hua will....”

Hyun Jong paused for a moment and said firmly.

“I will not accept the proposal of the Alliance Lord.”

At that moment, Jang Ilso halted his body there.

Jang Ilso’s gaze, completely frozen as if he had forgotten to breathe, would not leave Hyun Jong.

But that lasted only for a moment, and soon, his face contorted horribly. It was truly a dramatic change.

“... what did you say?”

As Jang Ilso growled, throwing off the formality he had been wearing like fancy clothes, the Mount Hua swordsmen who had been guarding Hyun Jong stepped ahead at once.

The negotiations broke down.

A terrifying killing intent rose on Jang Ilso’s face.

At that very moment.

“But...”

Hyun Jong continued speaking in a calm voice.

“I will release the Black Dragon King.”

Jang Ilso stared at Hyun Jong. He did not immediately understand Hyun Jong’s intentions.

“Please remember.”

“.... What?”

“Mount Hua does not negotiate with the Four Precepts Alliance.”

“....”

“The reason we are releasing the Black Dragon King is because we planned to do so from the start.”

“You planned to do so?”

“Yes.”

“Reason being?”

When Hyun Jong slowly turned his gaze, there was Namgung Do-Wei, who was gripping his sword so tightly that his hand turned white.

“This revenge is not for Mount Hua.”

“....”

“Mount Hua came here at Namgung’s request. It wasn’t to gain fame nor to shake up the murim world. We simply could not turn away from the request of one person.”

Jang Ilso stared blankly at Hyun Jong as if he had been hit in the back of the head. This must have been the first time in his life that he had seen such an expression on his face.

“Therefore, it is only right that the revenge against the Black Dragon King be carried out in the name of the Namgung family. Mount Hua has no right to snatch it away from them.”

“....”

“Then I will release the Black Dragon King. And you, too, the Hegemony Lord, please return now. This is not the place for you or us to stay.”

“Ha... Hahaha.”

At that moment, Jang Ilso’s face distorted strangely. It seemed like he was smiling, or maybe he was disappointed, or probably angry.

Jang Ilso, who had been looking at Hyun Jong like that as if he had seen a ghost, slowly turned his head.

Chung Myung.

He was smiling brightly at Jang Ilso.

Eyes filled with mockery.

Those eyes seemed to be saying:

Did you, who used to act like the whole world was under your feet, manage to anticipate this situation?

Tak!

Jang Ilso unconsciously raised his hand and covered his face.

Instinctively, he thought that he shouldn’t show the expression he was making to others.

He was the leader of the Ten Thousand People Clan and the Alliance Lord of the Four Precepts Alliance.

He was someone who shouldn’t distort his face just because of Mount Hua’s sect leader.

At that moment, Chung Myung opened his mouth.

“The sect leader has ordered it, so the Black Dragon King will be released as per the wish. But.”

Clench.

Chung Myung infused qi into the foot, stepping on the Black Dragon King’s head. A groan, which seemed like it would die off, escaped from the Black Dragon King’s mouth.

Chung Myung, who had been listening to the groan with an expressionless face, glared at Jang Ilso.

“I don’t trust you.”

“....”

“The Black Dragon King will be released after we arrive on the Northern lands.”

Jang Ilso glared at Chung Myung silently. His eyes were filled with a vivid killing intent.

“So get lost now. My patience has its limit.”

Jang Ilso laughed softly.

His shoulders, which had been trembling gently, soon began to shake violently. However, strangely, no laughter could be heard at all.

Silent madness.

Those who were watching the scene clenched their fists in inexplicable eeriness.

Jang Ilso, who had been laughing for a long time, slowly took his hands off his face. His face had returned to its usual calm expression.

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso slowly turned his head and looked at Hyun Jong again.

“Sect leader.”

“Go ahead, Alliance lord.”

“I believe that the sect leader would definitely keep his word.”

Hyun Jong nodded.

“I will.”

Jang Ilso nodded as if he was satisfied.

“You said you wouldn’t negotiate with the Evil Sect... that is the position of the sect leader. I am a person with dignity, so I cannot just follow the words of the sect leader. I will keep my word.”

Jang Ilso slightly raised his hand. Then, two ships from the fleet waiting behind began to approach Plum Blossom Island.

When Mount Hua’s swordsmen saw this, their faces hardened, and Jang Ilso twisted his lips.

“An empty ship.”

“...”

“Those who have accomplished great things are returning home in glory, so it is not right to be so shameless and do this. Think of it as my favor.”

Mount Hua’s disciples looked at Jang Ilso with eyes full of suspicion.

In their minds, the names Jang Ilso and Favor didn’t fit together.

However, Hyun Jong simply bowed deeply and showed his respect.

“Thank you.”

“...”

Jang Ilso looked at Hyun Jong, who bowed his head towards him without a word.

Jang Ilso, who had been staring at Hyun Jong for some time, slowly opened his mouth.

“Mount Hua’s Sect leader. Heavenly Friends Alliance’s leader.”

The voice was calm, but it spread with a strange resonance.

“... Hyun Jong.”

Jang Ilso nodded slightly. It wasn’t the exaggerated look he always showed.

“I will remember the name properly.”

“... it isn’t such a great name for the Hegemony Lord to remember.”

“Heheh.”

Jang Ilso smiled softly and turned around without any regrets. He slowly walked toward his small boat behind Chung Myung.

He inevitably approached Chung Myung. His eyes and Chung Myung’s eyes met.

“... this time.”

Jang Ilso opened his mouth with a bitter smile.

“I got hit. Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.”

Chung Myung’s mouth twisted.

“This time?”

A smile, which strangely resembled Jang Ilso’s but was clearly different, appeared, and Chung Myung’s mouth opened.

“You are talking so well when you barely managed to save your life and return.”

“...”

“Get lost.”

Jang Ilso shook his head slightly as if he had no choice and passed by Chung Myung.

The moment his body moved past Chung Myung and as his face was no longer visible to anyone...

Krkrkrk!

The rings on his hands tightened as they touched, making a cracking sound.

As if the vengeful spirits were screaming in pain.

Step. Step.

As he strode up to the small boat, it moved on its own and headed to the river.

‘Mount Hua and... Hyun Jong.’

Jang Ilso’s face turned blue and ghostly.

On the surface, it was a result that wasn’t too bad for him. In any case, he prevented the death of the Black Dragon King and left him with a huge debt.

Of course, even if the crisis itself was caused by Jang Ilso and the Ten Thousand People clan’s actions, the fact that he saved the Black Dragon King’s life didn’t change.

To those who did not understand this conversation, it would seem that Jang Ilso went into the enemy’s land, Plum Blossom Island, alone and rescued the Black Dragon King with just his three-inch tongue without shedding a drop of blood.

Jang Ilso’s reputation would surely rise even higher.

Yes. On the surface. Just the surface.

But now, Jang Ilso was filled with an inescapable sense of humiliation.

‘I...’

Jang Ilso’s face contorted like an evil spirit.

‘Is this the life I begged for, thanks to the mercy of a guy whose name I couldn’t even remember? Me? Jang Ilso?’

Hyun Jong was a being that didn’t even exist in his calculations.

Up until Mount Hua and the Tang family entered Plum Blossom Island, with the Black Dragon King in danger, everything happened just as he had calculated.

However, what happened after that was completely different from what he had thought.

If it was Mount Hua’s Sword Saint—Mount Hua’s Sword Saint who valued Mount Hua’s disciples more than his own life and never let go of profits he could hold in hand—he would never give up the negotiation proposal Jang Ilso had made.

That was definitely not a reckless offer for Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

He could return alive without sacrificing a single disciple of Mount Hua and gain the reputation of rescuing Namgung from Plum Blossom Island.

With this achievement alone, the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Mount Hua could gain a justifiable advantage over Shaolin and the Nine Great Sects.

This was an offer that no one with any sense could refuse.

Especially someone as astute as Mount Hua’s Sword Saint; he had no choice but to join forces with Jang Ilso, regardless of his personal feelings. By any means necessary!

That was why Jang Ilso was able to head to Plum Blossom Island without an escort.

He believed Mount Hua’s Sword Saint would never turn down the offer.

But...

‘Hyun Jong.’

Hyun Jong rejected his negotiation offer.

The man, Hyun Jong, dismissed the offer, which was absolutely impossible to refuse if one had even a slight understanding of what was beneficial.

That was something Jang Ilso could not comprehend, no matter how he looked at it.

And objectively speaking, at that moment, Jang Ilso was as good as dead.

Jang Ilso was nothing more than a fool who overestimated his own intellect and placed himself in danger when he underestimated the variable called Hyun Jong, and his proposal was rejected.

Even so, it was just a stroke of luck that he survived.

Jang Ilso, who had held everything in his hands and manipulated it as he pleased, seemed to have escaped with his life for reasons even he couldn’t understand.

“Heheheheh.”

Jang Ilso’s eyes once again showed a vivid killing intent.

“... Hyun Jong.”

The reason a madman was called a madman was because he committed acts that defied common sense.

In that sense, Jang Ilso may have seemed like a madman to them, but in Jang Ilso’s eyes, Hyun Jong was the true madman.

A crazy person whose mind he couldn’t understand or predict.

Such a person was the head of Mount Hua and the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. He had the unwavering support of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

Jang Ilso licked his lips with his tongue. His lips, red as blood, took on a slightly more sinister hue.

“That is why... the world is interesting.”

Jang Ilso twisted his lips forcefully, suppressing the burning sensation in his stomach as if he had swallowed charcoal.

However, his eyes grew even more somber.

“Hoooo.”

“Phew...”

The moment Jang Ilso left Plum Blossom Island, a collective sigh of relief escaped from the mouths of the Mount Hua disciples.

Some wiped the sweat from their foreheads, while others shook their heads vigorously.

The pressure Jang Ilso exerted was terrifying.

Just meeting him made them feel suffocated as if their skin was being sliced by a sharp blade.

Why was that person the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance? They could feel it in their very bones every time they encountered him.

But that was why...

‘Sect leader.’

Naturally, their eyes, looking at Hyun Jong, were filled with deep trust.

That Jang Ilso...

Hyun Jong didn’t back down an inch when facing Jang Ilso, the Hegemony Lord and ruler of the Southern Lands. No, it wasn’t merely a matter of not backing down.

Hyun Jong didn’t confront Jang Ilso forcefully, nor did he repel him by raising his qi.

He simply faced Jang Ilso calmly, as he usually did.

Who could do that?

They felt a renewed admiration for him. How great a person their sect leader was.

“Thank you for your hard work, Alliance leader.”

Those words came from none other than Tang Gunak.

This meant Tang Gunak must have thought the same as they did.

“No, lord.”

Hyun Jong shook his head.

“I was sweating profusely, trying to calm my trembling legs. The Hegemony Lord is indeed formidable. How can someone be like that...?”

Tang Gunak smiled faintly.

‘Such a great man.’

Anyone who saw Jang Ilso’s back as he turned around would feel the same way as Tang Gunak.

Who in the world would dare send Jang Ilso away like that?

“This is truly amazing...”

“I am sorry, but I think we should talk later, Lord.”

At that moment, Hyun Jong’s gaze turned to the Namgung family.

“Since the Hegemony Lord has promised us, there will be no more attacks. First, we should quickly move Namgung’s injured soldiers to land and treat them.”

“Yes, Alliance Leader.”

Tang Gunak rarely experienced interruptions in his speech.

But there was not a single trace of displeasure on Tang Gunak’s face at that moment.

This was because Hyun Jong’s anxious heart was conveyed clearly, worried that more of the Namgung family’s swordsmen might die from serious injuries.

“Move the wounded to the ship! We will return to the Northern lands.”

“Yes!”

Mount Hua’s disciples responded loudly with the same expressions. Interestingly, even the Sichuan Tang warriors joined in with raised voices.

Tang Gunak snickered at the sight.

Perhaps even his family members had clearly acknowledged Hyun Jong as their leader at this moment.

‘You have gained a lot.’

It was something they started impulsively, but he truly gained a lot.

Fame, legitimacy, achievements.

Right. So many things.

At that moment, Hyun Jong, who was in front of him, ran to support the Namgung family’s swordsmen as if he were scared.

Seeing that, Tang Gunak nodded.

‘But the greatest thing we gained here might be that person.’

Thinking about that made him curious again.

Tang Gunak turned his head.

He saw the back of Chung Myung, who was glaring at the Ten Thousand People clan across the river, with one foot stepping on the unconscious Black Dragon King.

‘Did you foresee this and come here?’

Of course.

Chung Myung wouldn’t answer it.

“Thank you for your hard work, Alliance Lord.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso, having climbed aboard the ship, smiled bitterly. Seeing that expression, Ho Ga-Myung asked with a hint of puzzlement, “Was there a problem?”

“A problem... well, I don’t know, Ga-Myung. Should I even call it a problem?”

“...”

“The Black Dragon King will be released, and the war has stopped, so everything I wanted is done.”

“Then why...”

Jang Ilso glanced back at Plum Blossom Island and shook his head.

“No.”

He cut off the words and soon gave an order with a calm expression.

“Tell the pirates not to touch those escaping from Plum Blossom Island and to send them away peacefully.”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

Ho Ga-Myung, after giving instructions to his subordinates, quietly observed Jang Ilso.

“What?”

“... I know I can’t dare guess what the Alliance Lord wants. But...”

“Tsk, tsk. Don’t beat around the bush. Why are you asking me this now?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes showed slight suspicion.

“It’s true the Black Dragon King is important. Without him, the pirates would lose control. It would be fortunate if we could absorb them safely, but the Lower District Sect and Black Ghost Fort wouldn’t just stand by.”

“Right.”

“But... even so, isn’t it too lenient to let them go like this? At least they could have caused some damage. They were surrounded and restrained on Plum Blossom Island.”

“Hmm, right. That’s true.”

“If I do that, what will...”

Jang Ilso spoke indifferently.

“The reason is simple. First, it’s more beneficial for me if those guys return safely to the Northern lands rather than die here.”

“...”

“Secondly...”

Jang Ilso snickered and looked back at Ho Ga-Myung. Then, suddenly, he said something unexpected.

“What do you think of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s face hardened the moment Jang Ilso mentioned Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

‘What do you think?’

If you asked someone who had observed everything on Plum Blossom Island, wouldn’t the answer be obvious?

“... a monster.”

“Right.”

Jang Ilso nodded quietly.

“He’s so suspicious you wonder if he came from a human’s stomach. The martial arts he showed me before are still vivid in my memory, but in just three years, he has...”

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint defeated the Black Dragon King single-handedly.

That meant Mount Hua’s Sword Saint should be recognized as one of the best martial warriors in the world.

Countless warriors had appeared throughout the long history of the murim world, but had anyone else reached this level at such a young age?

If one focused on martial arts alone, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint far surpassed Ho Ga-Myung’s knowledge and common sense.

“That’s why he’s dangerous. Extremely so.”

“Yes, he is dangerous.”

Jang Ilso curled his lips into a smile.

“But Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is dangerous not because he’s strong.”

Ho Ga-Myung didn’t understand what he meant.

“... What do you mean?”

Jang Ilso turned his head slightly and glanced at Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, Chung Myung.

“A strong person is just difficult to handle, not entirely impossible. If you can’t win alone, two can fight him, and if two can’t win, three can join.”

That’s the way of the Evil Sect. Ho Ga-Myung nodded in agreement. Jang Ilso continued.

“They say those who are strong are not truly strong. Those who know how to survive are the strong ones. But the problem is...”

He narrowed his eyes and trailed off for a moment.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is someone who knows how to survive.”

“... Uh?”

Jang Ilso’s face was faintly filled with dread.

“Ga-Myung.”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

“Do you really think Mount Hua’s Sword Saint jumped into Plum Blossom Island without any plan?”

“...”

“No. Not at all. A devilish guy with dozens of snakes in his stomach wouldn’t act on impulse. Even if he started on impulse, he must have finished his entire calculations while running here.”

“T-that is...”

“There they come.”

Jang Ilso gestured toward the river. Ho Ga-Myung turned his gaze in that direction, eyes wide.

At a glance, dozens of ships appeared.

“T-those are...?”

“They must be the ships called by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint to escape Plum Blossom Island.”

“B-but he already gave orders to the pirates to take over all nearby ships?”

“Yes. But isn’t there one guy skilled enough to get through all the interference and get ships like that?”

“... Uh?”

Jang Ilso snickered.

“The guy who sticks to the Justice faction like a bat and hasn’t even shown his face yet.”

Ho Ga-Myung bit his lip.

“... Im So-Byeong.”

“Right. That guy.”

Jang Ilso chuckled. But unlike him, all the color drained from Ho Ga-Myung’s face for a moment.

So, the guy called Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, while running all the way to Plum Blossom Island to save the Namgung family, arranged a fleet through the Green Forest King, Im So-Byeong, to escape safely?

“But the bandits...”

“There’s no need to fight the pirates. If you just spread out the ships, those guys can cross over like on a plank.”

“....”

Cold sweat ran down Ho Ga-Myung’s back.

Everyone gathered on the Yangtze River were famous warriors. However, among all of them, how many would have noticed the many hidden arrangements behind this battle?

“T-then?”

Ho Ga-Myung’s face turned pale as if he realized something.

“Did you enter Plum Blossom Island alone, even though you knew Im So-Byeong was steering the ships?”

“Right. Is there any reason why I shouldn’t?”

“N-no...”

“That’s the same either way. No matter how great that guy was, he couldn’t know exactly when the ship would arrive. You can’t gamble on a ship that may or may not come.”

Ho Ga-Myung let out a long sigh as he watched the fleet approach through the waves.

But what was even more bizarre was that Jang Ilso, who had barely escaped death by a narrow margin, was somehow smiling brightly.

“Do you understand now why I’m just letting them go?”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

Since they had prepared the ship, they would have been able to escape from the island even if they suffered damage. If that had happened, the Black Dragon King would have lost his life.

‘That would clearly be our loss, too.’

In that case, it was better to return with the Black Dragon King and let them go.

“I don’t know how long it’s been since I’ve felt this way.”

“... Alliance Lord.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso looked at Plum Blossom Island with a subtle expression once again.

“Ga-Myung.”

“Yes.”

“Send them what you’ve prepared. Since it looks just as I wanted, I should be giving them a gift.”

“I will.”

Jang Ilso waved his hand as if annoyed and walked slowly. His eyes, raised to the bow while pushing against the blowing river wind, settled quietly on Plum Blossom Island.

“In this world, if you gain something, you also lose something.”

His red lips curved into a smile.

“Do you know what you lost now?”

It doesn’t matter whether you know or not. It isn’t something you can do anything about even if you do.”

“He’s coming fast, that useless human!”

Chung Myung, clinging to the railing, gritted his teeth as he watched the approaching fleet. If that moron had arrived just a moment earlier, he could have cut off Jang Ilso’s head.

“Tsk.”

Now, Plum Blossom Island was quite far. The pirates surrounding the island’s outskirts were just watching them board the ship and escape.

With the Black Dragon King held hostage by Mount Hua and Jang Ilso’s words, they had no choice but to stay still.

At that moment, a person approached Chung Myung.

“Taoist.”

“Hmm?”

Turning his head, he saw Namgung Do-Wei walking unsteadily.

He walked precariously in front of Chung Myung, took a deep breath, and bowed his head. It wasn’t a formal greeting but a simple bow.

This was the courtesy shown by Namgung Do-Wei, the human being, not Namgung Do-Wei, the Lord.

“I am... truly grateful.”

“...”

Chung Myung stared at Namgung Do-Wei with an expressionless face.

Then, the Five Swords, a little distance away, started whispering among themselves.

“Why is he doing that?”

“Leave him alone. How many times has he heard someone say thank you in his life? I’ve only heard ‘you’re a piece of shit’ and nothing else.”

Those bastards!

Just as Chung Myung was about to glare at them, Namgung Do-Wei raised his head.

“Thanks to the Taoist, the Namgung family was able to continue our name. How do I ever repay this favor...?”

“Favor comes later.”

“... Uh?”

“The first thing you shouldn’t forget is resentment.”

Chung Myung glanced to the side and saw the Black Dragon King lying unconscious.

“How about now? There’s no need to keep the promise to the Evil Sect bastards anyway, right?”

It meant he could kill the Black Dragon King if he wanted.

But Namgung Do-Wei shook his head without hesitation.

“No.”

“Uh?”

Though struggling, Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes didn’t waver.

“Killing the Black Dragon King here won’t be the end of Namgung’s revenge. It would be nothing more than venting my anger. One day, I will defeat the Black Dragon King with my own strength and honor the souls of the dead.”

Chung Myung nodded in satisfaction. Then Namgung Do-Wei added something unnecessary.

“Because I’m sure my father would want that too.”

When Namgung Hwang’s name came up, the ship fell silent.

Chung Myung nodded slightly and lightly patted Namgung Do-Wei on the shoulder.

“Right. That’s right.”

At that moment, Namgung Do-Wei bit his lip.

“Chung Myung!”

“Uh?”

“A ship is approaching us.”

“What?”

Chung Myung kicked the deck and ran to the other side.

But the approaching ship was a small boat with only one person, despite his surprise.

It was the very same boat Jang Ilso had been on just moments ago.

“... What is that?”

Chung Myung frowned.

Fortunately or unfortunately, Jang Ilso was not on the ship. He had been spotted by Jang Ilso’s side a few times.

The moment the boat reached within arm’s length of Chung Myung’s ship, a loud voice called out.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint!”

Instead of answering, Chung Myung simply watched.

“This is a gift from the Lord.”

The person on the boat grabbed a large object loaded on the raft.

‘Coffin?’

Chung Myung’s eyes widened slightly as the large coffin was thrown onto the ship.

Tak!

Chung Myung silently accepted the coffin.

Ho Ga-Myung, who had been glaring at him, turned the boat without another word as if his work was done.

A strange silence descended on the ship.

Chung Myung carefully set the coffin down and looked at Namgung Do-Wei. Namgung Do-Wei must have guessed something, too, as he slowly opened the coffin lid with trembling hands.

And...

“Lord!”

“Kuaaaak! Lord!”

“Eukkkk!”

The moment the coffin was opened, the Namgung family warriors burst into tears.

Namgung Hwang.

He was in a miserable state, but the body inside the coffin was definitely his.

“Father! Father! Kuaak...”

Namgung Do-Wei collapsed, sobbing as he held the body.

He had thought the body would sink helplessly beneath the cold river water, his heart breaking at the thought of leaving the Yangtze River without even collecting it.

But Namgung Hwang’s body had returned to him in a coffin unexpectedly.

Chung Myung, watching the Namgung family and Namgung Do-Wei with dark eyes, turned his gaze toward the ship in the distance.

‘... That damned bastard.’

So disgusting.

The day he would understand Jang Ilso would never come. They would only be running in parallel lines forever.

But...

“Once in a while, you have to do something humane.”

Chung Myung spat out the words and turned away.

On the ship heading toward the Northern Lands, the sorrowful sobbing continued for a long time.

The members of the Namgung family who had retrieved Namgung Hwang’s body wept and collapsed from exhaustion. They had already been pushed to their limits, making it nearly impossible to endure any longer.

The Tang family cared for them with all their might.

“How are they now?”

Tang Gunak glanced back at Hyun Jong with calm eyes.

“There are many seriously injured, but fortunately, no one is expected to lose their life.”

“... That is quite fortunate.”

Hyun Jong sighed.

Though he called it fortunate, he couldn’t help but question whether the situation truly deserved that label.

The Namgung family had paid too high a price.

They had lost their lord and the elders all at once. Moreover, only a third of those who entered Plum Blossom Island had survived. Considering that nearly all of the Namgung family’s troops had reached the island, this was a loss that would be difficult to recover from.

Hyun Jong looked at Namgung Do-Wei, who had lost consciousness. Though he appeared tired and injured, even with his eyes closed, it was clear he was young.

The burden was too heavy.

He probably wasn’t thinking that far ahead now. However, once the sadness subsided and he regained composure, reality would begin to sink in.

The biggest problem was that almost all the elders—the backbone of the family—were gone. Although Namgung Myung was still alive, he wouldn’t be able to assume the role of family lord.

And that wasn’t the only problem.

Even if Namgung Do-Wei was a family genius capable of leading the Namgung family again, he was too young. It would take at least ten years before he could replace Namgung Hwang.

The predators of Kangho would not leave the Namgung family alone until then.

Hadn’t Mount Hua already experienced this once?

The brilliant reputation of their past was actually detrimental to a powerless sect because it attracted more challengers seeking that fame.

A huge loss and a break with the previous generation.

That would be...

Lost in thought, Hyun Jong shook his head.

It wouldn’t happen like that.

He couldn’t just stand by and watch them suffer.

“Please look after them.”

“Don’t worry.”

Tang Gunak smiled slightly.

His tone, as he spoke, was like someone entrusting his disciple to a physician. His eyes were filled with concern and affection. To Hyun Jong, whose relationship with the Namgung family was only a small connection, this sincerity was remarkable.

The more he looked at Tang Gunak, the more he thought he was amazing.

No, Hyun Jong wasn’t the only one who was amazing.

Tang Gunak glanced at the Mount Hua disciples who were gazing at the Yangtze River from the railing.

Their side was truly impressive.

Of course, he knew Mount Hua had grown stronger.

If those trained by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint over the past three years hadn’t improved, that would have been strange. Yet, the way they held their swords surprised even Tang Gunak, who could gauge their strength.

What kind of training did they undergo to grow so quickly?

It was natural for their internal qi to strengthen and their techniques to become more refined. Don’t all warriors dedicate themselves to that?

But their strength couldn’t be explained so simply.

Well, they were amazing people.

Tang Gunak smiled slightly, a bitter edge to it, and turned his gaze back to the patient he was caring for. To him, it was simply surprising and amazing—and, on the other hand, a source of pride.

However, this was possible because Tang Gunak knew and understood the Mount Hua sect to some extent. For the Gentle Crow Beggar, who had unintentionally accompanied Mount Hua to Plum Blossom Island, it wasn’t an easy situation to grasp.

Was Mount Hua always this strong?

To his eyes, there was no significant difference between when they entered Plum Blossom Island and now. Mount Hua and the Tang family had literally rescued the Namgung family from the island without suffering major damage.

“... This is troublesome.”

The leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance was surely Mount Hua. However, that did not mean Mount Hua was the strongest sect within the alliance. Everyone had thought so—at least until now.

Mount Hua’s leadership stemmed from being the core of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and a sect that embodied the alliance’s values. In other words, the actual strength of the Heavenly Friends Alliance had been carried by the Sichuan Tang family.

But...

Not anymore.

If what happened on the Yangtze River became known to the world, no one would continue to think that way.

Now, Mount Hua has become the true leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance. It had grown into a great sect comparable to the leading sects among the Nine Great Sects.

He wondered how those sects would accept this situation...

Gentle Crow Beggar’s gaze shifted to the riverside.

There, Shaolin, Kongtong, and a few Beggars Union disciples remained, having set up camp.

The abbot stared blankly at the two approaching ships. His eyes twitched faintly.

What was this?

Those who had entered the island on their own feet were now leisurely leaving. What was even more incomprehensible was that the pirate ships, which should have blocked their path, were actually opening the way.

What nonsense was this?

His lips trembled.

That didn’t mean they intended for them to stay there forever.

Once Mount Hua and the Tang family had set foot on Plum Blossom Island, there were two possible outcomes.

Either they would be annihilated along with the Namgung family on the island, or they would be wiped out.

After much difficulty, they had succeeded in escaping Plum Blossom Island, but he expected to die one by one in the cold Yangtze River under attacks from pirates and the Ten Thousand People clan.

In the former case, he could do nothing. But if the latter happened—which was once in a thousand, once in ten thousand—he would naturally jump into the Yangtze River to help them. He couldn’t bear to watch them die there while he escaped.

However, the situation was completely different from what he had expected.

“How...”

As if the dizziness he had felt was now a thing of the past, his eyes grew extremely complicated.

How on earth did all this happen?

The judgment he had made while standing here was absolutely correct. No matter how much he reviewed it, there was not a single mistake. Entering Plum Blossom Island was no different from committing suicide.

But now, all of that meant nothing.

Since Mount Hua and the Tang family—that was, the Heavenly Friends Alliance—had entered Plum Blossom Island on their own feet and rescued the Namgung family, his cool judgment would be seen as a foolish miscalculation, and his patience until the end would have been cowardly indifference.

There was no way to avoid the situation where the Nine Great Sects would be criticized by the world. No, the Nine Great Sects were not the problem right now.

“...”

The abbot glanced behind him. The Shaolin monks lined up behind him and looked at the two approaching ships with expressionless faces. They didn’t even glance at him.

He clenched his fists without realizing it.

These were the people who had kept their positions even when Hae Bang left. This meant they had not lost faith in the abbot even then. But now, these people were turning away from him.

These foolish bastards!

At that moment, the abbot was silently praying to calm his boiling emotions.

“... Abbot.”

Jong Ri-Hyung, who had been watching the situation from the side, looked at the abbot with trembling eyes. He was also a sect leader and could not be so foolish as to not know where this was heading.

“W-what are you going to do now?”

“...”

The abbot was as silent as a clam. The saying that even if you had ten mouths, you would have nothing to say must have been made for such a situation.

“If things were going to be like this, wouldn’t it have been better for us to join Mount Hua when they headed to Plum Blossom Island? If we go back like this, what will the world think of us?”

When the abbot remained silent despite the torrent of reproaches, Jong Ri-Hyung raised his voice in frustration.

“Abbot!”

“Calm down!”

The abbot looked back at him with an angry face. Jong Ri-Hyung flinched at the gaze.

The abbot pressed his lips together and said,

“... They will arrive soon. Should we raise our voices here?”

“But...”

“Just wait.”

The ship was just about to reach the riverbank.

“It isn’t over yet.”

Light flowed from the abbot’s eyes.

Meanwhile, the ship carrying the Heavenly Friends Alliance finally reached the riverbank.

Kwang!

The sound of the ship’s front lightly touching the sandbank echoed like thunder.

Gulp.

In the heavy silence, where even the sound of swallowing was audible, everyone’s eyes turned to the ship.

“Carry the injured carefully!”

“Yes!”

The Sichuan Tang warriors in green robes jumped off the ship, carrying the survivors of the Namgung family.

Jong Ri-Hyung closed his eyes tightly.

They really saved them.

From afar, it was hard to tell if the Namgung family was truly on that ship. But at that moment, it became an undeniable fact that the Heavenly Friends Alliance had rescued the Namgung family.

What should they do now?

It was something to be happy about—something to be extremely happy about and praised for.

But Jong Ri-Hyung couldn’t feel that way. He felt disgusted with himself for not being able to purely rejoice that those who had suffered had returned from the dead.

Perhaps the Kongtong disciples lined up behind him felt the same.

Meanwhile, the survivors of the Namgung family were carried off one after another from the ship. The Tang family members, who found a flat place to lay down the unconscious Namgung family, checked their condition once more.

After that, Mount Hua’s swordsmen finally appeared.

Dressed in black robes, they jumped off the ship hurriedly and lined up in place. No one spoke or looked away.

Perhaps it was nothing more than an act of ignorance toward those present. No matter how heroic they were, it was clearly rude not to even look at Shaolin and Kongtong.

However, those standing there were grateful. They simply did not have the confidence to meet their gazes.

At that moment, Hyun Jong, the sect leader of Mount Hua, appeared behind them.

Step.

The abbot, who had been silently watching, moved first.

He walked meaningfully toward Mount Hua, bowed deeply to Hyun Jong, and gave a round of applause.

“Thank you for your hard work, sect leader.”

Hyun Jong smiled quietly.

The two had experienced a conflict that could be called extreme before coming to the Yangtze River, but their faces now showed no trace of ill feelings from the past incident.

“You are absolutely right. I just did what I had to do.”

The abbot was silent for a moment, then bit his lip.

What Hyun Jong said was a common humble remark.

However, the phrase “what I had to do” really struck a nerve with the abbot. Didn’t that imply that Shaolin hadn’t done what should have been done?

Of course, Hyun Jong had no ill intentions. The abbot knew that Hyun Jong was not someone who would deliberately sneer or mock. However, the lack of ill intentions made the abbot feel even more ashamed.

His eyes flashed with intense light.

“But.”

Slowly straightening his back, he looked meaningfully at Mount Hua and the Tang family, then turned his gaze toward the fleet of pirates still occupying the Yangtze River.

“I think you need to explain one thing.”

Hyun Jong narrowed his eyes for a moment.

“Explain?”

“Yes, sect leader. It might seem trivial, but it is important.”

“... What is it?”

The abbot glared at Hyun Jong with cold eyes.

“If my eyes do not deceive me, it seems that the pirates and the Ten Thousand People clan opened the way for the Heavenly Friends Alliance to escape. Isn’t that right?”

Hyun Jong fell silent.

It wasn’t because of the question itself but because he instinctively felt the sharpness behind the abbot’s words.

“I don’t understand how such a thing is possible with the common sense of a small monk.”

“Abbot, right now...”

“So, you will have to explain that to me, sect leader.”

The abbot spoke, squinting his eyes.

“I am asking whether the Heavenly Friends Alliance was in collusion with the Evil Sects on Plum Blossom Island.”

The air along the riverbank froze for a moment.

In the bone-chilling silence, Baek Cheon stared at the abbot with a blank expression.

‘Now...’

What on earth was that man talking about just now? Did he mention collusion? Between the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Evil Sect?

Baek Cheon’s eyes reddened. His blood surged, and his heart boiled with anger.

“This...”

Just as he was about to stride toward the abbot with blazing eyes, someone grabbed his shoulder and pressed him down.

He clenched his teeth and turned to see Un Geom standing there. Un Geom’s jaw was tight, but he shook his head to stop Baek Cheon.

“...Stop.”

“Sasuk.”

“It’s up to the sect leader to handle this.”

Yet Un Geom seemed barely able to contain his own anger; his one remaining hand trembled on Baek Cheon’s shoulder.

Mount Hua wasn’t the only one surprised by the abbot’s words. Jong Ri-Hyung, the sect leader of Kongtong, also looked at the abbot with a face that was not just shocked but bewildered.

‘Collusion.’

What on earth did that mean? Surely, he had been watching what happened on Plum Blossom Island.

At that moment, Hyun Jong broke the silence.

“I...”

His face showed neither anger nor resentment. He simply faced the abbot with an emotionless expression. But anyone who knew even a little about Hyun Jong would understand how much anger he was suppressing.

“It’s hard for me to understand what the abbot is asking right now.”

There was no emotion in his words. It was difficult to hear Hyun Jong speak like that.

The abbot smiled broadly.

“That’s exactly what I asked.”

“...”

“Common sense tells us there’s no reason for the pirates not to attack the Heavenly Friends Alliance as they leave the island.

“The reason is simple: we have the Black Dragon King hostage.”

“Is that so?”

The abbot glanced at the ship, surprised.

‘No wonder.’

He nodded, realizing that Mount Hua’s Sword Saint hadn’t disembarked yet.

“I understand what you mean.”

“Did that answer your question?”

The abbot slowly shook his head.

“No.”

At that answer, Hyun Jong’s face hardened.

“That isn’t enough, sect leader.”

The smile vanished from the abbot’s face.

“Surely, you’re not trying to claim that the sect leader has that much faith in those people, right?”

“Abbot.”

For the first time, anger appeared on Hyun Jong’s face.

Depending on one’s perspective, the abbot’s question might be justified. The process of their departure from the island must have seemed unnatural from the land. Hyun Jong could understand their doubts.

But there was one reason he couldn’t hold back his anger.

Malice.

Because the abbot’s words were filled with malice. They weren’t merely expressing doubts and expecting a convincing explanation. No matter the answer, they were intent on throwing them into a dirty situation and trampling them.

“We risked our lives to save Namgung.”

“I would like to thank the Heavenly Friends Alliance for their cooperation in rescuing the Namgung family. But...”

The abbot turned to Mount Hua, lined up behind Hyun Jong, to the young swordsmen glaring at him with murderous eyes.

“Who lost their life?”

“Abbot!”

Hyun Jong shouted, but the abbot didn’t even blink.

“Isn’t this a situation where they stormed a place occupied by the world’s most powerful forces and pirates and escaped without a single casualty? Isn’t that unnatural?”

Unbearable anger appeared on Hyun Jong’s face.

What on earth was this insane man talking about?

“Common sense tells us there must have been some kind of agreement between the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the Four Precepts Alliance. Otherwise, are you saying the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s strength was such that it didn’t suffer a single loss against the Four Precepts Alliance?”

The abbot’s face was stern. Listening to his serious words and stern reprimand, which seemed free of selfish intentions, one might wonder if Mount Hua had done something wrong.

As expected, the disciples of Kongtong and the Beggars Union, who had been silently listening, began to exchange glances. They started to think that what the abbot said wasn’t entirely baseless.

“There’s just one thing I can say.”

Hyun Jong spoke with a stern face.

“Mount Hua did not compromise with them. It’s just that we took the Black Dragon King hostage, so they couldn’t do anything to us.”

“Hmm?”

The abbot’s eyes widened.

“That means...”

Hyun Jong’s face paled for a moment upon seeing those eyes. He realized what was coming next.

“Does this mean the Heavenly Friends Alliance agreed to spare the Black Dragon King, a villain hated by heaven and earth, in exchange for leaving Plum Blossom Island?”

Hyun Jong shut his eyes tightly.

The reason Mount Hua decided to spare the Black Dragon King was because the revenge belonged to the Namgung family. But who would believe that story?

To those who heard the abbot’s absurd claims, it would sound like they begged for their lives in exchange for releasing the most horrible man.

The current Kangho was too harsh to believe Hyun Jong’s words.

“That’s too much!”

At that moment, someone suddenly raised their voice. It was the Gentle Crow Beggar.

The abbot’s eyes turned to him.

“If you can’t believe what the Heavenly Friends Alliance says, then believe what I say, not them. I watched everything that happened on the island. From what I saw, the Heavenly Friends Alliance did not collude with the Evil Sects!”

Gentle Crow Beggar glared at the abbot and declared,

“I swear on my name.”

But the abbot just shook his head.

“Your name is worth nothing.

“What did you just say...”

“Isn’t it you who gave up the position of Elder of the Beggars Union, which would prove your words?”

Gentle Crow Beggar trembled with anger that showed on his face.

‘This insane man!’

But he couldn’t refute it. How could he argue against something so illogical?

“And!”

The abbot frowned.

“Such a nameless person’s ears and eyes mean nothing. If what I fear is true, the world will be thrown into great turmoil. Can you really take responsibility for those words?”

“Listen here, abbot!”

“It’s not your place to step forward!”

The abbot pressured the Gentle Crow Beggar.

No matter how much he had lost his name, the abbot was the head of Shaolin. When he pressed with such determination, Gentle Crow Beggar couldn’t move.

“Be careful. If they were plotting with the Evil Sects, then perhaps their entry to Plum Blossom Island was part of their plan. One shouldn’t judge solely based on what happened there.”

“You...”

The Gentle Crow Beggar shivered.

But he couldn’t speak further. He sensed the abbot had no intention of listening.

‘How could this be?’

It wasn’t that he didn’t understand. He knew why the abbot was acting this way. That made holding back his anger even harder. His stomach felt like it was boiling.

“I will ask again.”

The abbot looked at Hyun Jong.

“Is it true that the Heavenly Friends Alliance colluded with the Evil Sects on the condition of releasing the Black Dragon King?”

Hyun Jong tried to remain as calm as possible. He had a gut feeling that showing anger would only give the abbot more ammunition.

“...It might look that way. But that isn’t collusion.”

“So, the sect leader considered the Black Dragon King’s life so important to Jang Ilso that he would release Mount Hua, the Tang family, and Namgung?”

“What is that...”

“And.”

The abbot’s eyes scanned Hyun Jong.

“It could also mean he begged for his life himself in exchange for saving the Black Dragon King, who is so important.”

“Abbot.”

Tang Gunak, who had been silently watching, stepped forward as if he could no longer tolerate the behavior.

“How can you call that collusion? By that logic, didn’t Shaolin also beg for their lives by making an agreement with them at the Yangtze River?”

The abbot smiled.

“Right, lord. That is correct.”

Tang Gunak’s expression hardened at that reaction.

“And in return...”

The moment he saw the abbot smile, Tang Gunak realized his mistake.

“The Wudang took responsibility by entering the Closed Gate. So I ask if this whole situation is no different from the previous Yangtze River massacre...”

His eyes were subtle, but his voice was clear.

“Which sect in the Heavenly Friends Alliance will pay the price for making an agreement with that Evil Sect?”

“This fucking dog...!”

Tang Gunak’s eyes reddened as he cursed. He understood the abbot’s intent.

It was no longer possible to restore Shaolin’s former honor. If the events at the Yangtze River spread, no one would think highly of Shaolin.

Meanwhile, the name of the Heavenly Friends Alliance would shine even brighter, to the point where Shaolin could no longer keep up.

If they were normal people, they would find a way to restore their honor and rise again. But the abbot’s plan was the opposite.

He wanted to drag Mount Hua down into the mud.

Words and actions were different.

Mount Hua invaded Plum Blossom Island, surrounded by the Evil Sect, rescued the Namgung family, and escaped without damage—a great achievement.

But what if rumors spread that Mount Hua colluded with the Evil Sect to spare the Black Dragon King in order to escape safely?

Dirt on a dirty person is hard to see, but dirt on someone as pure as white snow is vivid.

Tang Gunak, realizing the abbot’s intentions, glared at him as if ready to kill. Unlike Mount Hua, the Tang family bore no ill will toward Shaolin. But at this moment, Tang Gunak’s eyes were no different from those of a mortal enemy.

He was fed up with the vicious malice.

The abbot smiled broadly.

“Don’t get so excited. I don’t want to believe the Heavenly Friends Alliance is foolish enough to collude with the Evil Sects over something like that.”

Tang Gunak frowned. After pushing people to the limit, what nonsense was he spouting?

“No matter how much I think about it, it doesn’t make sense. Aren’t the names of Mount Hua, the Tang family, and Namgung too prestigious to be exchanged for the life of just one—the Black Dragon King? The value of those three sects cannot be traded for the life of a mere leader.”

“Do it moderately...”

“Perhaps not only the life of the Black Dragon King...”

At that moment, the abbot’s voice spread clearly across the entire Yangtze River.

“If they had made a secret pact in advance to give more, what would it be?”

“You crazy bastard!”

In the end, Tang Gunak couldn’t hold back and let out a loud roar.

They risked their lives to enter Plum Blossom Island. Why did they have to be treated like this?

But this forced statement was surprisingly effective. The eyes of the Nine Great Sect disciples guarding the abbot began to shift oddly.

Perhaps it was natural.

They lived with the pride of being disciples of the Nine Great Sects, but this time, they had to watch Mount Hua operate on the Yangtze River.

Because they were weak?

That couldn’t be. No matter how strong Mount Hua became, weren’t they the Nine Great Sects? They couldn’t be much weaker than Mount Hua.

But how should they interpret the situation where Mount Hua defeated the Evil Sects and rescued the Namgung family without others even lifting a finger?

No matter how much they thought about it, it was too unnatural.

They only suspected because of what had happened around Mount Hua so far, but if another sect did something similar, they would have already concluded there was a conspiracy on the Yangtze River.

There was no blood on Tang Gunak’s face.

‘Damn it.’

They probably didn’t understand. The obvious timing was difficult in their eyes.

The abbot was now cleverly provoking the pride of those watching the Yangtze River.

Was it a Three-Way Battle?

A minority’s argument doesn’t gain strength. But if this many people present had doubts, their voices would surely spread with power.

‘What should we do?’

How can we wisely overcome a situation where the most authoritative person in the world is determined to drive us out, and the most trusted people look at us with suspicion?

Even Tang Gunak, the greatest in the world, couldn’t speak rashly.

“Sect leader.”

At that moment, the abbot looked at Hyun Jong with a strange smile as if to drive in the last wedge.

“I will ask one last time. Did Mount Hua really make no agreement with the Evil Sects?”

Hyun Jong looked up at the sky, feeling lost.

‘It’s scary.’

It was so intense and terrifying. He thought he already knew Kangho, but this horrible malice made him shiver again.

“I will say it again!”

Just as Hyun Jong was about to raise his voice with force,

“...So what if we colluded?”

A cold voice pierced the air.

Everyone on the riverbanks turned toward the source.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, Chung Myung, was slowly walking toward them, his expression colder than the winds of the North Sea.

Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, Chung Myung.

That name certainly resonated within Kangho.

Whether advocating for Justice or Evil, wasn’t it the name of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint that everyone in Kangho couldn’t help but know?

But even for those familiar with that name and the symbolism Mount Hua’s Sword Saint held in the murim world, this was clearly an awkward sight.

Shaolin, Mount Hua, Sichuan Tang family.

The leaders of these three sects—none holding reputations as being fierce—were fiercely fighting against each other in a suffocating situation. The name of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint still seemed too small to be involved in such a conflict.

Furthermore, the rude words and actions he displayed, which were hardly worthy of being spoken, wouldn’t have been odd if he had been punished for disrupting talks between sects.

But Mount Hua said nothing to Chung Myung.

It was odd, yet perhaps understandable. Among those present, few could boast that they truly knew what kind of sect Mount Hua really was.

That left those watching with another real question.

‘Why isn’t the Tang family saying anything?’

It was the Tang family’s nature. Tang Gunak was explosively expressing his anger, and a third-class disciple had intervened in such an urgent situation. Wasn’t this enough to insult the Tang family?

Even though Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was close to the Sichuan Tang family, considering the unity of the Tang family—difficult for any sect in the world to match—it was not strange for Tang family members to harden their faces and attack Chung Myung. No, it was natural for them to do so.

But the Tang family was silent. They simply stared at Chung Myung, who stepped forward with hardened eyes. Even his gaze was filled with utmost trust.

It was natural for those watching to feel embarrassed.

‘It couldn’t be otherwise.’

The Gentle Crow Beggar slowly nodded after seeing the flustered faces of the Nine Great Sects.

He fully understood their bewilderment. If he had not witnessed with his own eyes what kind of person Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was at Plum Blossom Island, he would have stood there with the same expression.

In other words, if they had seen Mount Hua’s Sword Saint defeat the Black Dragon King at Plum Blossom Island firsthand, their reactions would have been completely different.

Chung Myung proved with his own hands that he was worthy of this conversation. The ranking was of utmost importance in the sect, but sometimes something else mattered more.

That was skill.

Who would dare say that someone who single-handedly took on and defeated the Black Dragon King was arrogant?

That was why the Gentle Crow Beggar could understand the reactions of both the Heavenly Friends Alliance, who looked at him with firm eyes, and the Nine Great Sects, who regarded him with suspicion.

Chung Myung, walking between the Heavenly Friends Alliance, opened his mouth after a long silence. His eyes stared straight at the abbot.

“What if we colluded?”

The abbot’s face, looking at Chung Myung, turned stern.

“What will you do?”

That sight was very symbolic.

The abbot was the abbot of Shaolin. It wouldn’t be an exaggeration to say he currently holds the highest status in Kangho.

The sight of a mere disciple of Mount Hua lashing out at the abbot of Shaolin made onlookers feel an extreme sense of alienation.

The abbot’s status, which had been met with gazes full of suspicion and disappointment, never collapsed. That status might have been invaded by a person who came in with mud on his feet.

“... Taoist.”

The abbot’s face darkened for a moment. However, he couldn’t show anger.

The man, Chung Myung, he knew was frivolous, but at the same time, every action had a hidden intention. He should never act according to Chung Myung’s wishes.

“That sounds very dangerous.”

That was why the abbot laughed instead—to show his affectionate side, embracing even a child as if he were a naked babe.

“Then, does the Taoist now admit that the Heavenly Friends Alliance and Evil Sect are in collusion?”

Chung Myung smiled.

“I told you.”

“...”

“So what are you going to do?”

The smile vanished quickly, and anger spread over the abbot’s face.

“You child!”

A voice loud enough for everyone to hear rang out.

“Don’t you know how important that is? Are you saying that?”

A stern reprimand. It was a tone showing superiority and making clear that the attitude of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint was not right for dealing with the Shaolin abbot.

No matter how right a story was, if it was not accompanied by appropriate attitude and manners, its light would fade, right?

But some people in the world do not attach importance to such things.

“Is it important?”

Chung Myung sneered.

“Right, it must be very important. To you.”

“See. Taoist...”

“But.”

Chung Myung’s cold voice cut off the abbot’s words.

“At least to us, none of that matters. The important thing was how to save the Namgung family.”

The abbot’s lips twitched slightly at the growling tone of words that sounded like a ferocious beast.

“While you were just watching those dying here, we jumped into the river ourselves and risked our lives to save Namgung. But what? Collude?”

Chung Myung’s eyes, filled with killing intent, turned to the abbot—no, more precisely towards the disciples hiding behind the abbot, trying to breathe slowly. They did not dare meet Chung Myung’s eyes and lowered their heads.

It wasn’t because they were afraid of his strength.

Those who worshipped justice and compromise could not deny that.

“I will ask again.”

“...”

“If we colluded, what are you going to do? Will you draw your sword and cut off all our throats?”

“Taoist, what I mean...”

“Try it.”

Chung Myung’s eyes, glaring at the abbot, dripped with killing intent.

“Do it.”

He bared his teeth like an animal, and the abbot clenched his lips at the force being projected.

In fact, when he pressed them, he didn’t have any plans for the aftermath. He just thought that if he pointed this out, he could reduce the criticism the Nine Great Sects would receive.

But when those who should have been protesting and making a fuss came out like this, he was speechless.

It was just as Chung Myung said.

If they had colluded with the Four Precepts Alliance, what would he do? Would he lead the Nine Great Sects and punish them?

At that moment, the abbot realized that the Heavenly Friends Alliance, whom they had never thought would be their opponent, had now grown to the point where they could stab them in the jaw.

Whether it was because the Nine Great Sects were in a state of self-restraint and had lost power or because the Heavenly Friends Alliance had grown beyond expectations and gained strength, the reason wasn’t so important.

What was important was that the Heavenly Friends Alliance was no longer afraid to fight them.

Look at this sight.

Even though Chung Myung was not hiding his killing intent and openly revealed it, Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak didn’t try to stop him. Didn’t this mean that even these two people with the most authority in the Heavenly Friends Alliance were not afraid to display hostility to the Nine Great Sects?

Not just that, the Heavenly Friends Alliance lined up behind him also glared with eyes that didn’t back down an inch.

When had he ever faced such hostility?

For the abbot, who had always been met with eyes full of admiration and trust, the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s hostility was too difficult to handle.

“Taoist.”

“It is disgusting. That ‘principle’ you keep talking about.”

There was no warmth in Chung Myung’s gaze.

The abbot probably wouldn’t think he was wrong. He started by trying to hold the Heavenly Friends Alliance back, but as he talked, he probably came to believe his logic was right.

He felt even more upset knowing that.

The shamelessness of not feeling an ounce of shame while talking about how their nobility was more important than those who were dying, the cruelty of turning a blind eye to those being stabbed to death while talking about great principles and values.

How many people had died under that logic?

Do not join hands with the Evil Sect. You cannot negotiate with those barbarians outside the land. A warrior must not defile himself even if he dies. How much blood has been shed on the ground by the logic of those high-ranking men who never shed blood on the battlefield but only talk from the rear?

What Chung Myung really couldn’t stand was that they didn’t take any responsibility for the sacrifice.

Collusion? Maybe. In their eyes, the agreement between Hyun Jong and Jang Ilso might seem like it.

Of course, they could refute it as much as they wanted, but there was no reason to do so.

Even if not now, if needed, they would collude whenever they wanted.

If it could save even one more disciple of Mount Hua, Chung Myung wouldn’t hesitate to lick the feet of Jang Ilso. Even if the world pointed a finger at him, he could laugh at them.

So, there was no reason to get angry over such a ridiculous thing.

“Say whatever you want to.”

“...”

“No matter what we say, it’s going to be like that anyway, right?”

Chung Myung’s lips curled into a snicker. That blatant insult pierced the abbot’s heart.

“So go ahead and say whatever you want. That we are colluding with the Evil Sect to take over Central Plains. That everything that happened on Plum Blossom Island was the work of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

Chung Myung glanced at the abbot and the disciples of the Nine Great Sects.

“Isn’t that your way?”

“Taoist! Is that something that comes out of the mouth of someone who worships righteousness?”

The abbot raised his voice as if he couldn’t listen any longer. However, even after hearing that scolding, Chung Myung’s face remained cold.

“Righteousness?”

Chung Myung sneered, twisting the corners of his lips.

“I don’t know about that.”

“... What did you say?”

“I don’t know about that. Righteousness, whatever... You guys can say whatever you want. I’m just doing what I have to do.”

Chung Myung coldly rebuked the abbot, who was momentarily speechless.

“Do you know how to avoid committing a crime?”

The abbot’s eyes filled with doubt at the unexpected question. When Chung Myung answered, he simply shut his eyes tightly.

“It’s to not do anything. Just like you guys who were here.”

A calm silence flowed along the river.

There was no need to judge right or wrong.

Even just looking at the disciples of the Nine Great Sects who couldn’t bear to look straight ahead and the followers of the Heavenly Friends Alliance who proudly raised their heads, it was clear who was right.

“One day, you might find yourself in the same situation as Namgung.”

“...”

“Say the same thing then. You have to do it by the rules. If you nod your head, saying that this is right even when your neck is to be cut off, I will at least acknowledge that belief.”

The bodies of the Nine Great Sect disciples trembled slightly.

“But if you cannot do that...”

The killing intent flowing from Chung Myung stabbed everyone’s hearts like a knife.

“Shut that disgusting mouth and get out. I cannot stand you anymore.”

“Taoist!”

The moment the abbot finally moved, Chung Myung glanced at him.

“....”

The abbot’s shoulders, which had been tense, sank as his gaze turned cold.

“You should have killed me three years ago.”

“You...”

“Right?”

Chung Myung smiled brightly and turned around without any regrets.

The abbot’s fingers trembled slightly.

“That...”

Watching Chung Myung turn away after speaking his mind, the abbot felt as if he had swallowed a hot coal.

But there was nothing he could do. He knew all too well how futile it would be to argue with Chung Myung once he had already turned his back.

“Mount Hua’s Sword Saint!”

The atmosphere shifted completely. Now, no one dared to challenge the words of Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

No matter how loudly the abbot shouted, here, his words carried no weight.

So, the abbot redirected his arrows at someone else, not Chung Myung.

“Is... is this what the Heavenly Friends Alliance stands for?”

He tried to steady his voice and glared directly at Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak.

No matter how much authority Mount Hua’s Sword Saint’s words held, he was not officially appointed within the Alliance. That meant his words could not represent the will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

Ultimately, the will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance was determined by Hyun Jong and Tang Gunak.

The abbot pressed them with his gaze.

“Answer me. Is this truly the will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance?”

Hyun Jong met his gaze calmly.

“It’s a bit much to call it the will of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, but I’m not certain...”

The abbot’s face twisted at the vague response.

“Sect leader!”

He tried to press him urgently, but Hyun Jong simply shook his head slowly.

“As that child said, even if given another chance, we would head to Plum Blossom Island without hesitation.”

“...”

“There’s not a shred of shame in what we did. If the world criticizes us, wouldn’t that be inevitable? From the start, everything we did wasn’t meant to be shown to the world, so we have no choice but to accept it.”

The abbot stared at Hyun Jong, eyes wide with disbelief.

What did all this mean?

Wasn’t this whole scandal supposed to be revealed to the world? Would they accept the criticism?

Could these words really come from someone who led a sect or an alliance?

“Abbot.”

Hyun Jong sighed.

“Nothing in this world is perfect.”

“...”

“That’s why nothing is completely right. So I can only ask myself: Am I truly on the right path?”

This probably wasn’t meant as a criticism of the abbot, but the abbot felt as if Hyun Jong was silently asking, “Do you really believe you are on the right side?” A deep discomfort gnawed at him.

Yet Hyun Jong’s eyes did not waver. In their depths lay his unwavering belief in his convictions.

“My heart tells me I did the right thing. So what does it matter what others think? Please do not force Shaolin’s way upon us. The Heavenly Friends Alliance will do what it believes is right.”

“...”

“I think that’s the answer. Then.”

Hyun Jong stepped back.

Of course, the abbot wanted to hold him there. He knew he couldn’t let them leave like this.

But he was powerless. Someone approached from behind Hyun Jong.

A figure whose once white robe was stained crimson with blood. A person limping forward steps heavy with sincere resentment.

No matter how urgent the abbot’s thoughts, he couldn’t ignore this person and stop Hyun Jong.

That person was none other than Namgung Do-Wei.

“... Disciple Namgung.”

Namgung Do-Wei stood before the abbot, eyes burning with resentment.

Why wouldn’t he be?

Shaolin had utterly betrayed Namgung’s hopes.

It would have been better if he had never appeared by the riverside, but to come all this way and watch Namgung be thoroughly trampled was unforgivable.

Of course, the abbot had his reasons, but would they matter to the Namgung family, who had endured such hell?

Even the abbot never thought he could be forgiven by Namgung. Yet when he faced Namgung Do-Wei—the Namgung family member who harbored the deepest grudge against Shaolin—he was left speechless.

While the abbot hesitated, Namgung Do-Wei spoke first.

“... Collusion.”

A hollow laugh escaped him.

“If what the abbot says is true, then our Namgung family saved their lives in exchange for collusion between the Four Precepts Alliance and the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“D-disciple.”

“What should we do?”

A murderous glare suddenly flared in Namgung Do-Wei’s sunken eyes.

“This pitiful life saved by their dirty collusion—should I commit suicide now and reclaim your honor?”

“Disciple Namgung. What I mean is...”

“Haha... Hahahaha.”

Namgung Do-Wei let out a twisted laugh.

“Those who should have died and fallen have come back to life for no reason and upset the abbot. I apologize for that.”

“That is not what I meant.”

“Really?”

Namgung Do-Wei’s serious expression, as if he had never laughed, pressed the abbot.

His eyes asked:

Did Shaolin truly want the Namgung family to return alive from that island? From the moment he gave up on rescuing them, didn’t he wish there were no survivors?

The abbot could not answer.

If Namgung Do-Wei had been the only problem, he could have suppressed him with his authority. But when all the Namgung survivors who had regained their senses looked at him as their mortal enemy, what could he say?

Even if he had ten mouths, he would have nothing to say.

“If you don’t want to see me, then it’s right for us to disappear.”

Namgung Do-Wei slowly looked around at everyone.

“As the young lord of the Namgung family... and acting lord with the official lord no longer present!”

His voice rang out majestically.

“From now on, the Namgung family will withdraw from the Five Great Families and pledge our friendship to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“N-Namgung disciple!”

A voice close to a scream escaped the abbot.

He had thought Namgung Do-Wei would naturally resent the Nine Great Sects, but how could he have known he would make such a bold declaration right then?

“This is not something to be decided emotionally!”

“Right, you should be rational. Rational like the abbot who thoroughly watched over Plum Blossom Island from here!”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei bared his teeth and spat those words at the speechless abbot.

“So when you claim that the Heavenly Friends Alliance is in collusion with the Evil Sect, please include this as well: the Namgung family has also become lackeys of those Evil Sect bastards.”

“...”

“Please do that, abbot.”

Namgung Do-Wei turned away as if unwilling to speak further.

The abbot’s face went pale.

‘T-this cannot be happening.’

The Namgung family alone could not be sent to the Heavenly Friends Alliance. No, even if they were to join, it couldn’t be now!

Who would believe this?

The moment the Namgung family joined the Heavenly Friends Alliance, everything the abbot had said would be rendered meaningless. Even his suspicions about the Heavenly Friends Alliance’s intentions would become a scandal!

All his justifications would benefit the Heavenly Friends Alliance, and the status of Shaolin and the Nine Great Sects would be utterly destroyed.

That must be stopped. That one thing!

“Disciple! Think again! Namgung family...”

“Enough!”

At that moment, someone grabbed the abbot’s shoulder.

Turning around, he saw Jong Ri-Hyung approaching, his face contorted with concern.

“This is not the time, abbot.”

“... Sect leader.”

“It’s not wrong to raise suspicion. But there is a time and place, isn’t there? Injured people are groaning over there. Suppose the abbot, who speaks of the Buddha’s mercy, prioritizes revealing the truth and saving Shaolin’s reputation over treating the wounded. How would the world see him?”

“You’re being too much!”

The abbot shouted, his face flushed red.

Jong Ri-Hyung bit his lip tightly. He wanted to say harsher words but held back.

No matter how distasteful, Shaolin was the leader of the Nine Great Sects.

Kongtong was one of the Nine Great Sects, and among them, Shaolin was the only sect worth siding with.

Whether he liked it or not, he had to maintain relations with Shaolin, so he couldn’t press further.

“... Just let it go, for now, abbot.”

Lowering his voice, he added,

“Aren’t there too many eyes watching?”

At those words, the abbot instinctively turned around. The beggars of the Beggars Union, standing behind Shaolin and Kongtong, watched the scene with bright eyes.

Their job was to prevent rumors from spreading worldwide. No matter how much the Beggars Union controlled information, they couldn’t block all eyes and mouths.

And no matter how he looked at them, their eyes were not favorable to him. If he pushed harder, who knew what words might spread?

‘Damn it.’
