Hyun Jong shrugged his shoulders and sat down at the top of the table. His eyes flashed with sadness for a moment, but fortunately or unfortunately, his disciples didn’t give him pitiful looks.

They just gave him looks that showed they understood everything.

‘Don’t give me that look. It makes me want to cry!’

What can I even do?

The reason is simple. Because he had given Mount Hua’s full authority over the finances to Hyun Young when they were living in poverty, he couldn’t take back the authority even after Mount Hua began to rake in money.

No matter how shameless Hyun Jong was, he couldn’t ask for the money back after he had left all the hard work to Hyun Young to handle the debts.

As a result, Hyun Jong was now the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance and the leader of Mount Hua, who criticized the entire Kangho but couldn’t avoid receiving occasional pocket money from Hyun Young instead of a full salary.

Hyun Jong wiped his eyes with his sleeve and refined his expression. He couldn’t continue doing that.

“I called you here to discuss future matters.”

The eyes of his disciples changed once he began to speak seriously. Hyun Jong realized once again that his authority had increased incomparably to the past.

Of course, when he and Mount Hua were at the bottom, these people had respected them too, but there was something different now.

“As you know, we have fallen out with the Nine Sects now.”

“Hmm.”

He thought this would happen, but it was something that couldn’t be helped.

Isn’t it the Nine Great Sects of the world?

No sect in the world would want to fall out with such a place. Even the Evil Sect would wish to avoid it.

“So we need to firmly establish our position from now on.”

Un Am asked, puzzled by Hyun Jong’s words.

“Sect leader, this might be presumptuous, but...”

“Speak.”

“If that is the case, wouldn’t it be better to discuss it with the Tang family Lord rather than with us? I think this is more of a matter for the Heavenly Friends Alliance than for Mount Hua.”

At those words, Hyun Jong shook his head.

“If you think about it rationally, that might be true. But I do not wish for that.”

“... why though?”

“Because before I am the leader of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, I am the sect leader of Mount Hua.”

Hyun Jong smiled broadly and looked at everyone.

“It is even more so when I cannot see the path that I should take. I have to discuss it, and I want to do it with you first.”

Everyone smiled at those words.

He seemed to be telling them that no matter how different their positions may be, Hyun Jong is still Hyun Jong. He was the sect leader who had warmly patted them on the back when they had been starving together.

“That is why, Chung Myung.”

“Uh?”

Hyun Jong looked at Chung Myung quietly and said.

“What do you think we should do from now on?”

“Hmm?”

Chung Myung scratched his cheek.

The disciples of Mount Hua all thought that Chung Myung would give them a unique answer.

Whenever they asked questions like this, Chung Myung would always suggest a new path with an idea that no one could ever think of. They naturally thought that this would be a repeat of that.

However, those thoughts were shattered the moment Chung Myung opened his mouth.

“I think the question is quite wrong, sect leader.”

“Hmm? The question was wrong?”

“Yes.”

Chung Myung nodded.

“That is the only way I can answer that question.”

He paused for a moment and looked at Hyun Jong.

The moment he saw those deep eyes, Hyun Jong’s body naturally gained strength.

“What does the sect leader want to do?”

“... me, you ask?”

“Yes, sect leader.”

When Hyun Jong hesitated slightly to answer, Chung Myung turned his head and looked around at the others.

“And I think we should ask the people gathered here what they want to do. The path Mount Hua wants to take should be decided by Mount Hua together. Before considering what we can do, I think we should decide on what we want to do.”

“....”

It wasn’t such a big deal. It was so obvious.

But that obvious statement had great meaning there.

‘That lone general...’

‘... Oh my.’

Most people looked at Chung Myung in surprise. However, Jo Gul did not stop there. He reached out and shook his head in front of Chung Myung’s face.

Chung Myung furrowed his brows slightly.

“... what?”

“No, I thought you ate something wrong. You don’t have a fever or something, right?”

“....”

Fortunately, before Chung Myung could act, Yoon Jong kicked Jo Gul in the side. Chung Myung spoke again, leaving Jo Gul struggling without a care.

“Let us talk about it together.”

Everyone waited for Chung Myung’s next words.

“About the path that Mount Hua should take in the future.”

His eyes were full of determination.

“Chung Myung.”

“Uh?”

Hyun Jong stared quietly at Chung Myung before opening his mouth.

“I’ve heard similar words from you a few times before. But today, it seems they carry a slightly different meaning than usual.”

Hyun Jong, the sect leader of Mount Hua, spoke with a deep weight in his voice.

“In any case, it seems you believe the conversation we’re about to have should be different from those we’ve had so far. Is that right?”

“Yes, sect leader. That’s correct.”

“Then.”

He paused for a moment before continuing.

“Is there a reason you think it should be different this time?”

Hyun Jong asked this to make clear to those who didn’t understand the situation that the upcoming discussion was important.

And it was indeed a reasonable decision.

... if the clapping of hands was normal.

“Why?”

“Right.”

“... I’m asking why?”

“....”

Hyun Jong sensed something was off and glanced at Chung Myung’s face, then quickly shut his eyes tightly. Chung Myung tilted his head to the side.

‘Why is he suddenly throwing a fit now?’

What on earth did Hyun Jong even do?

“Sect leader.”

“...r-right.”

“I don’t know where to start or how to explain this.”

“U-uh?”

Hyun Jong wanted to say, ‘If you don’t know how to explain it, then there’s no need to bother trying.’

But since he was perceptive, he couldn’t bring himself to say those words.

“Sect leader might have thought I was insane all this time.”

“... you realized it?”

“....”

Hyun Jong realized he had made a slip of the tongue and quickly changed his words.

“N-no! That isn’t it! How could I think of you like that? No. NO! That isn’t it! Such nonsense.”

“... Ahem.”

Chung Myung coughed softly as if to let it go this time. However, Hyun Jong didn’t miss the trembling in Chung Myung’s eyes.

“Sect leader.”

“Y-yes.”

“What was Mount Hua like when I entered?”

“... Uh?”

“Wasn’t Mount Hua a sect worse than the Beggars Union, where only beggars gathered?”

“T-that much is....”

“It’s the truth.”

Hyun Jong’s timid rebuttal was immediately cut off by Chung Myung.

“The Beggars Union had more money than Mount Hua. The beggars ate better than us.”

“....”

Well... that wasn’t entirely wrong.

“From what I hear, Mount Hua was worse than the Beggars...”

“What’s wrong with that? Beggars don’t have debts to pay.”

“....”

Hyun Jong, speechless, just coughed in vain.

“Anyway!”

Chung Myung glared.

“Now that I think about it, was Mount Hua even a sect where people lived back then?”

“....”

“When I joined, I saw that all the sect’s items were sold off and gone!”

Martial Hall leader Hyun Sang and White Blossom Boarding Leader Un Geom turned their heads in surprise.

“The sect had no money and was full of debt!”

Hyun Jong and Hyun Young looked up at the distant ceiling.

“The kids didn’t train and just played around like thugs!”

“W-why are you bringing all this up now?”

“Jo Gul shut your mouth.”

“... Yes, sahyung.”

Chung Myung snorted and shouted.

“In that situation, what? Direction? Discussion?”

“....”

The more his eyes flashed, the more everyone tried to avoid eye contact.

“People were about to get beaten up by bastards from the Southern Edge sect right then! What was done about that?”

“R-right. That happened.”

“I took care of the Southern Edge sect bastards, then the Wudang ones started a fight, and now we’re going against Shaolin and the Precepts Sects Alliance!”

“C-Calm down, Chung Myung.”

Hyun Jong was startled by Chung Myung’s face, so red it looked like it might explode.

Thinking back, it wasn’t wrong. It’s a memory now, but when Chung Myung entered, was Mount Hua an ordinary sect? If Chung Myung hadn’t found Mount Hua’s treasures, then they would have all been kicked out of the main sect and had their signboard taken down.

“I’m getting angry again just thinking about it! What kind of sect is...!”

Everyone bowed their heads. Thinking about it, it wasn’t their fault, but they felt awkward about it all.

“Phew! Phew...!”

Chung Myung took a deep breath and barely calmed his excitement, then looked back at everyone.

“Anyway, up until now, we’ve been busy dealing with what was right in front of us. Otherwise, the sect would have been ruined.”

“Right, right.”

Hyun Young nodded as if he understood everything.

“Right! A person has to at least make ends meet to do something. What can you do when you’re going to starve tomorrow?”

“Ugh. As expected, the elder knows everything.”

“Ahem. I do know. You really did something great.”

“Heheh. What did I even do?”

Chung Myung smiled, scratching the back of his head.

But at this time, no one cast a disgruntled look at Hyun Young, who was showing favoritism. First of all, it was true that the guy did calm down.

Chung Myung raised his head and looked at everyone.

“Until now, it was a matter of dealing with immediate problems. But from now on, it isn’t like that. As you know, Mount Hua has become a fine sect.”

If other sects had heard this, they might have thought Chung Myung was being humble. Mount Hua’s current position in Kangho was that significant.

Of course, there were still weaknesses—there weren’t enough senior disciples and skilled warriors to take responsibility for the sect—but they had built up a reputation and fame more than enough to overcome that.

“That’s why I want to ask you, sect leader.”

Chung Myung looked straight at Hyun Jong and asked.

“What do you think, sect leader? What kind of ties do you want to have with the Nine Great Sects?”

“Hmm.”

Hyun Jong let out a heavy sigh.

Although it was a long time ago, Chung Myung’s words carried a simple meaning behind them.

Mount Hua had not had the luxury of choosing anything. Most of what Mount Hua had done so far had been a desperate attempt to survive, and they had been busy resolving crises.

But...

‘Not anymore, though.’

The real meaning behind Chung Myung’s words was clear. Mount Hua now had some freedom. It was only a thin thread of leeway, but at least they could catch their breath for a moment.

So now they could discuss and plan.

About the direction Mount Hua should take.

Hyun Jong looked around quietly. Everyone was watching him, waiting for him to speak.

“This is just my will....”

That’s why Hyun Jong insisted on saying this. Chung Myung told him he was the sword of Mount Hua. And the only one who could wield that sword was Hyun Jong.

But that was just Chung Myung’s will.

Hyun Jong did not want that. Rather than being the one who wielded the sword called Chung Myung, he wanted to be someone who would move forward with Chung Myung. No, he wanted to be someone who would walk with not just Chung Myung but everyone in Mount Hua.

‘Mount Hua is the sect that came this far.’

Hyun Jong, having made up his mind, opened his mouth.

“Chung Myung.”

“It seems we can no longer move forward with the Nine Great Sects.”

Then Chung Myung asked with a blank expression.

“Why?”

Hyun Jong thought for a moment and then said.

“Actually, it wasn’t my intention to distance myself from them. As the sect leader of Mount Hua, what I wished for most was to restore the past glory of Mount Hua and become a respectable successor to the previous sect leader.”

“Yes.”

“That’s why I secretly hoped to return to the Nine Great Sects. Although our ties have deteriorated somewhat, I thought that if they reached out first, it wouldn’t be bad to restore good relations.”

Chung Myung nodded.

It was something that could be done. Chung Myung was radical enough to tremble at the thought, but if Chung Mun sahyung were here, wouldn’t he have thought the same as Hyun Jong?

“But I realized this through this incident: the path they want to take is so different from ours.”

A slightly gloomy look crossed Hyun Jong’s face. He must have felt great disappointment through this incident as well. Nevertheless, his tone remained gentle and cautious.

“I’ll say it again, this is just my opinion. Of course, I am the sect leader of Mount Hua. However, Mount Hua is not a sect where the sect leader decides everything. No, it should never be that way.”

Hyun Jong asked in a voice as calm as flowing water.

“What do you all think?”

Hyun Young snorted.

“Now, even if we want to be friends with them, will that old, ignorant man accept it?”

“.... That old, ignorant man is a bit...”

“Aren’t the two leaders of the Nine Great Sects Shaolin and Wudang?”

“Yes.”

“The Shaolin abbot attacked us head-on in front of everyone, and the Wudang sect leader got beaten up in front of everyone by us! Do you think they’ll get along with us? Even if they went insane, they wouldn’t!”

“...”

Everyone’s eyes turned to Chung Myung.

“What? Why!”

However, Chung Myung straightened his shoulders as if he had done nothing wrong.

“So don’t waste your efforts. Those men probably never thought about getting along with us in the first place. So why should we care?”

“Hmmm.”

Un Geom, who had been listening silently, smiled brightly.

“Your words might be a bit harsh, but I agree with the elder. We’ve come too far to turn back now.”

“....”

“And even if they wanted that, as the sect leader said, their path is different from Mount Hua’s.”

“Yes!”

“That’s right, sect leader!”

Words of agreement poured out here and there. Hyun Jong nodded quietly.

This wasn’t because Mount Hua had grown stronger. Mount Hua was a sect that knew better than anyone the sorrow of being weak. That’s why they didn’t want to go down the wrong path.

“Then.”

This time, Hyun Jong asked everyone.

“What kind of sect do you think Mount Hua should be?”

“....”

This time, no one could respond easily. It was too deep a question to answer simply.

At that moment, Hyun Jong’s gaze turned to none other than Yoon Jong.

“Yoon Jong.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

“Answer now. What kind of Mount Hua do you want to see?”

Yoon Jong’s gaze deepened at Hyun Jong’s question.

“Sect leader, I’m sorry, but... I don’t know anything that great.”

“Hmm. Is that so?”

When Hyun Jong asked with regret, Yoon Jong opened his mouth again.

“But I think I know what I should do.”

There was a lack of confidence in his voice. Everyone’s gaze turned to Yoon Jong.

“When the young lord of Namgung came to ask for help this time, I wanted to help him more than anyone else. And I was really proud of Mount Hua for rushing to the Yangtze River to help him. I have experienced many things in Mount Hua, but I have never felt prouder to be a disciple of Mount Hua than at that time.”

“... is that so?”

“Yes, sect leader.”

Yoon Jong nodded slowly.

“That’s why I will do what I feel is right. Even if it’s difficult and arduous, I will not turn away from those who ask me for help. Just as Mount Hua went through hardships in the past, I will not hesitate to help those who are struggling and exhausted.”

A gentle smile appeared on Hyun Jong’s lips.

“A place where you listen to your conscience and simply act on it—that’s the kind of place I think Mount Hua is.”

When Yoon Jong finished speaking, Hyun Jong nodded.

“I have my Mount Hua and Yoon Jong will have Yoon Jong’s Mount Hua.”

He looked into the eyes of each of Mount Hua’s disciples one by one.

“But there’s no need for all of that to come together into one opinion. If each of us achieves the Mount Hua we want to see, it will eventually become a better place than anyone imagined.”

Hyun Jong trailed off slightly and closed his eyes, mumbling.

“I believe so.”

A heavy silence settled over the room.

The deep resonance of Hyun Jong’s words dug into everyone’s hearts.

‘Right. That will do.’

They would face countless difficulties in the future. But if they don’t turn away from what they know now and push through, they will overcome those challenges someday.

And when that happens, they and the world will all know what kind of sect Mount Hua truly is.

Hyun Jong was about to speak again.

“Is that alright?”

“....”

When he slowly opened his eyes, Chung Myung’s eyes shone with a meaningful expression.

“The Mount Hua each of you wants to achieve?”

“....”

“Is that so? Then I don’t have to hold back either?”

“....”

No.

Let us find another way, Chung Myung.

“Well then, first things first...”

Hyun Jong drew everyone’s attention, signaling it was time to address the practical matters.

“Baek Cheon.”

“Yes, sect leader.”

“How do you think Shaolin will act moving forward?”

Baek Cheon was momentarily speechless, meeting Hyun Jong’s gaze. Hyun Jong had never asked him this question directly before, especially not in front of everyone.

But Baek Cheon quickly regained his composure.

“As Chung Myung mentioned earlier, Shaolin probably won’t move from this river for a while. For them to move, the pirates would have to withdraw first, but the pirates are still holding their fleet.”

“Hmm, that sounds right.”

Hyun Jong nodded in satisfaction. At that moment, Jo Gul quickly interjected.

“Of course! The bald king of Shaolin values his pride immensely!”

“...Right.”

Now, even if Jo Gul called the abbot of Shaolin, a bald king, no one would dare correct him.

“Then the key question is when the pirates will move.”

Hyun Jong pondered deeply before speaking.

“Isn’t the Black Dragon King injured? I doubt he can endure staying there forever, even for treatment.”

“Yes, I think so.”

“I agree.”

“So you’re saying this standoff won’t last long?”

As everyone’s thoughts converged, Chung Myung spoke up.

“Is that really so?”

“Uh?”

Hyun Jong looked at Chung Myung with a puzzled expression.

“Do you see it differently?”

“Yes, sect leader. At first glance, it seems the Black Dragon King should retreat due to his injury, but I think otherwise now.”

Baek Cheon asked, confused.

“Why? From the Black Dragon King’s perspective, he needs to recover quickly. In an Evil Sect where pride runs deep, the leader’s skills are paramount.”

“On the contrary.”

“Uh?”

“If the Black Dragon King still firmly controlled his pirates, then what Sasuk said would hold true. But there’s someone else involved.”

Baek Cheon immediately realized who Chung Myung meant and groaned.

“Jang Ilso...”

“The Black Dragon King has lost control of the pirates. The pirates are a sect under the Four Sects Alliance, and their leader is right there. Since the Black Dragon King is injured, it wouldn’t be surprising if Jang Ilso temporarily took command of the fleet.”

“...That makes sense.”

“And...”

Chung Myung smiled mockingly.

“That bastard wouldn’t just let this situation slide. He probably found an excuse to push the Black Dragon King off the front lines, aiming to fully consume the pirates’ power.”

Everyone couldn’t help but agree. How could Jang Ilso, the embodiment of greed, miss such an opportunity?

“Will the Black Dragon King just sit back and watch?”

“The Black Dragon King?”

“Yes. Given his nature, he would never quietly watch Jang Ilso devour his power.”

“...”

At that moment, Chung Myung looked at Baek Cheon with a pitying expression. Baek Cheon flinched and protested.

“What? Why!”

“Sigh... this is why you’re Sasuk. Oh my, Dong-Ryong.”

“Sigh, don’t start now, you punk!”

Chung Myung shook his head.

“Well, you’ll see soon enough.”

He glanced around at everyone and continued.

“Anyway, the standoff will probably last longer than we expect.”

“...Why?”

“That would take too long to explain.”

Chung Myung chuckled as he chose his words carefully.

“The thoughts of those with tainted hearts tend to be similar.”

The Five Swords still looked confused, while Chung Myung just smiled softly.

His eyes had grown cloudy, and his face was so gaunt he was barely recognizable.

The commanding presence he once had was gone, even after washing his eyes. His dark complexion now evoked only pity.

The Black Dragon King stared blankly ahead, focusing on Jang Ilso, who sat cross-legged on a large chair.

“Where...”

Jang Ilso’s lips, which had been curled in a smile like a crescent moon, slowly parted.

“Are you feeling alright now?”

This man was always threatening, like a venomous snake.

The reason the Black Dragon King found him especially dangerous now wasn’t just because he had lost an arm.

“Thanks for your concern...”

The Black Dragon King paused, lowering his head.

He didn’t want to show it, but his face must have been filled with anger, hatred, and fear.

“... I’m recovering quickly.”

“Thank God.”

Jang Ilso leaned back in his chair, looking down at the Black Dragon King with a relaxed expression.

Just days ago, the Black Dragon King had stood on equal footing with Jang Ilso. Though Jang Ilso held the position of Alliance leader and the allied leaders of the Four Precepts Alliance, the Black Dragon King hadn’t been forced to bow to him.

That was because Jang Ilso’s position wasn’t seized by force.

Though the Black Dragon King was gradually losing initiative to Jang Ilso, he still had room to counterattack.

Before things deteriorated this far.

“You’re getting better...”

Jang Ilso murmured as if recalling the Black Dragon King’s words.

“Yes, you should recover quickly. Then you can seek revenge and reclaim your reputation, right?”

“...”

“For now, focus on healing. It’ll take time to get used to wielding the sword with your left hand.”

He thought he heard laughter.

Though there was no trace of laughter in Jang Ilso’s voice, the Black Dragon King kept hearing the hallucination of Jang Ilso laughing in his ears.

No, he must be laughing.

It might not show on his face or in his voice, but Jang Ilso was surely bursting with anger.

“Don’t worry. I’ll handle the small matters now. For the time being, step back and focus on regaining your strength.”

Crack.

The Black Dragon King clenched his teeth.

Step back?

He hadn’t even faced the pirates since being released by Mount Hua. Stepping back from the front lines required at least some discussion and orders. Yet now, he was completely isolated from the pirates.

One thought never left his mind.

Was all of this really a coincidence?

Jang Ilso’s timely support, Mount Hua and Namgung’s advance to Plum Blossom Island, and his own near-death experience without intervention...

Was it all really just by chance?

At first, he dismissed it as nonsense. Even Jang Ilso couldn’t have predicted the Black Dragon King’s defeat by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint. But everything had played into Jang Ilso’s hands, and he hadn’t missed the opportunity.

Yes, he believed that.

Until he saw Jang Ilso’s eyes.

The moment those eyes, like a venomous snake stalking prey, met his, the Black Dragon King realized everything.

“Is there a problem?”

Jang Ilso’s voice was calm. The Black Dragon King barely swallowed his anger. Anger that can’t be expressed isn’t anger—it’s the sorrow of the weak.

“...No, Alliance leader.”

“Hmmm.”

Jang Ilso snorted softly.

“Alliance lord...”

He muttered for a moment and slowly stood.

The accessories on his body clinked sharply.

Anyone who knew Jang Ilso would recognize the sound. But to the Black Dragon King, it was creepier than a ghost’s howl from hell.

Step. Step. Step.

Jang Ilso approached slowly.

The Black Dragon King’s shoulders trembled.

He wasn’t afraid of death. If it were just death, he would have accepted it with dignity.

But Jang Ilso was not one to be satisfied with mere death. He would use death to strip everything away—the pirates, his honor, everything he had built—leaving nothing but ashes.

Step. Step.

Finally, Jang Ilso stopped just a step ahead of the Black Dragon King. He smiled—the gentlest smile imaginable.

“Why are you shaking?”

“...”

“I will...”

A languid voice slipped from his red lips.

“Do you think I will kill you?”

The Black Dragon King closed his eyes.

He finally understood why others feared Jang Ilso so much.

Tigers didn’t usually fear snakes; they avoided them because their venom was deadly and offered no benefit. That’s why snakes and tigers coexisted.

But...

What if the snake was so large it could swallow an entire tiger? What if it wasn’t a snake but a giant poisonous scorpion with its massive mouth wide open?

It would be strange for a tiger not to fear such a creature.

“Don’t worry, I won’t do that.”

Jang Ilso continued with a soft smile.

“I think I’m gentler than people believe... but people fear me. Do you know why?”

His tone shifted completely as if addressing his subordinates kindly. Yet the Black Dragon King couldn’t utter a word of protest.

“In their hearts...”

Jang Ilso’s eyes glowed blue.

“There is hostility.”

“...”

“They say a dog doesn’t fear its master. It finds him strong and happy. It fears the whip of discipline, but what it offers its master is simply happiness. What a dog truly fears is the enemy. Do you understand?”

“...Alliance Lord.”

“Then I’ll ask.”

A sharp smile appeared on Jang Ilso’s lips.

Clap.

The rings hidden in his sleeves made a sharp sound.

It wasn’t loud, but to the Black Dragon King, it sounded louder than thunder.

“What’s inside your heart now?”

The Black Dragon King opened his eyes and looked at Jang Ilso.

Seeing those eyes—bright yet infinitely gloomy—something inside him broke.

“I...”

“Yes. Answer me.”

“I-I...”

His body slowly bent.

Thud!

His knees hit the ground.

With a face full of fear and horror, he trembled and lowered his head with difficulty.

“All I have is... respect.”

Jang Ilso’s gaze turned cold as he looked down at the kneeling Black Dragon King. After a moment of silence, he slowly reached out his hand.

The white hand, adorned with jewels of five colors on the fingers, touched the Black Dragon King’s head. Instantly, the Black Dragon King’s body flinched and spasmed briefly.

“Hmmm.”

A smile appeared on Jang Ilso’s lips as he let out a short snort.

“Right.”

The white hand slowly stroked the Black Dragon King’s head.

“Such a good boy.”

The moon in the sky slowly slipped behind the clouds. A terrible darkness quietly descended upon the Yangtze River.

“I salute you, Alliance Lord!”

Ho Ga-Myung bowed deeply to Jang Ilso.

“You’re acting like this again.”

Jang Ilso waved his hand dismissively.

It really was nothing.

The moment the Black Dragon King was defeated by Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, the outcome had already been decided. No, to be precise, it might have been sealed from the moment the Namgung family set out for Plum Blossom Island.

All Jang Ilso did was steer this flow as he wished. So it was only natural that he earned the Black Dragon King’s loyalty.

“Now, the pirates will definitely be under the command of the Alliance Lord.”

“It took a long time.”

Jang Ilso shook his head, sounding fed up.

“It took three years to get my hands on the pirates, the weakest force in the Four Precepts Alliance. It was tedious.”

Ho Ga-Myung looked at Jang Ilso with eyes full of respect.

Three years.

It might seem like a long time, but if it took three years to completely absorb those pirates, who would dare belittle that period?

This was an achievement beyond words.

“What about the pirates?”

“They’re following without complaint. Even if they have issues, they can’t express them in this situation.”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso gazed toward the riverside, where lighted ships embroidered the Yangtze River.

“About two or three...”

“...Uh?”

“Catch two or three people with good positions and make an example of them. Somewhere, everyone can clearly see.”

Ho Ga-Myung hesitated for a moment before speaking.

“Right now, there’s no one who has committed a crime worth...”

“Tsk, tsk, Ga-Myung, Ga-Myung. Why are you always so honest?”

“...”

“It doesn’t matter whether there’s a criminal or not. What matters is that there has to be an example.”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

“It’s foolish to solve a problem only after it occurs. Truly smart people don’t give themselves room for problems to arise. That’s how people are. When someone does something different, others tend to feel the urge to do the same. Once a rebellious spirit is ingrained, it can be suppressed, but won’t disappear.”

“...”

The best thing is.“

Jang Ilso slowly ran his lean fingers down his lips.

“To prevent them from even having a rebellious spirit. To do that, we need to scare them rightfully. Do you understand?”

“Yes, Alliance Lord. I will kill a few people who can serve as examples and hang them on the sails.”

But Jang Ilso sighed deeply again.

“Ga-Myung...”

“Yes?”

Ho Ga-Myung looked puzzled. Jang Ilso shook his head.

“When did I tell you to kill them? I’m not that horrible a person.”

“Then...”

“We need to save them.”

Jang Ilso spoke calmly.

“Hang them alive. It would be better if their skin were peeled. Let everyone clearly see them suffer for days, neither alive nor dead.”

Ho Ga-Myung’s eyes widened slightly.

“Dig out their flesh here and there, sprinkle some salt on it, and make them swarm with bugs. Every time they fall asleep with groans of dying songs, they should feel like they absolutely do not want to die like that. They should think they’d rather have their throat cut, sink to the bottom of the river, and become fish food than die like this.”

The peaceful and calm voice, as if talking about everyday life, strangely felt several times more cruel than an ominous tone.

“There are many ways to gain loyalty, Ga-Myung. But right now, there’s nothing we need more than fear. Since they obeyed even a fool like the Black Dragon King without question, it’s enough to show them stronger power and crueler hands.”

“...I will do so.”

Jang Ilso quietly smiled at the simple answer.

“Right, then that will do.”

Ho Ga-Myung swallowed hard.

Actually, Jang Ilso’s methods weren’t particularly cruel. It wasn’t a bad thing in the Evil Sect to brutally kill an enemy.

The problem was that these were now Jang Ilso’s subordinates, whom he would rule.

“Alliance Lord, may I ask you something?”

“Hmm?”

“...What do you plan to do with the Black Dragon King?”

“Why ask about the Black Dragon King?”

“Do you plan on keeping him alive?”

“Hmm.”

Jang Ilso looked at Ho Ga-Myung as if amused.

“What do you think?”

“I think it would be better to kill him. A man like the Black Dragon King will never be truly loyal to others. He’s a man who will show his teeth to the Alliance Lord at any time.”

“An untamable wolf then?”

“I don’t know if ‘wolf’ fits that bear-like man, but in any case, he will never be tamed.”

“Ga-Myung.”

“Yes.”

“Do you know how to tame an untamable wolf?”

“...I don’t know.”

“Just pull out the teeth.”

Jang Ilso said calmly.

“Pull out the teeth, pull out the claws, and if he still won’t listen, cut off the legs.”

“...”

“Then, even if he can’t be as loyal as a dog, at least he won’t bite or scratch me, right? Then he’ll be no different from a dog.”

“But what’s the point of being the Black Dragon King if he ends up like that?”

“Tsk, tsk, Ga-Myung.”

“Yes, Alliance Lord.”

“The important thing is that I subdued the wolf and am using it.”

“...”

“It doesn’t matter whether the wolf has sharp teeth or lethal claws. What matters is that the wolf who didn’t listen to anyone is trapped in my cage, leashed, and submitting, right?”

“...I understand what you mean.”

“Anyone can kill the Black Dragon King and take the pirates. But that’s just the appearance of a new Black Dragon King. It’s different from putting the entire pirates under your feet. That’s something not just anyone could do.”

Jang Ilso’s gaze shifted to the side, not toward the Yangtze River, but toward the vast lands of the Southern Lands.

“Even if others don’t know, at least two of you will understand the meaning. That’s enough for now.”

Jang Ilso smiled seductively.

Obtaining the pirates meant Jang Ilso had acquired at least half the power of the Four Precepts Alliance.

Originally, Jang Ilso was the leader of the Four Precepts Alliance, but if two or more of the remaining clans joined forces against him, he would have no way to deal with them. However, from this moment on, Jang Ilso had the power to fight the entire Four Precepts Alliance.

“It’s a shame I couldn’t repay him for what I got, but I think I’ve made quite a profit.”

Jang Ilso mumbled as if talking to himself, and Ho Ga-Myung carefully opened his mouth as he watched him laugh.

“Alliance Lord.”

“Uh?”

“Then from now on...”

“Just leave it alone.”

“...Really?”

“Yes.”

Jang Ilso reached out, picked up a bottle of alcohol, took a sip, and stared blankly across the river.

“There are things in the world that require time. Let’s just wait for those pirate people to accept me. The presence of the enemy on the other side will make that possible.”

“... Wouldn’t Shaolin be the first to pull away, though?”

“As if they can.”

Jang Ilso waved his hand.

“Shaolin is now facing the worst situation. If they back down like this, they can no longer prevent their fall. Listen carefully, Ga-Myung. The world doesn’t revolve around power alone. No matter how strong Shaolin is, once a name is lost, it won’t come back easily.”

“But being here doesn’t necessarily mean they will gain anything.”

“Instead, war clouds will start to gather.”

“...War clouds?”

Jang Ilso looked at Ho Ga-Myung with a strange smile.

“Why did I sign a three-year treaty during the previous Yangtze River massacre?”

“Because you needed time to control the inside of the Four Precepts Alliance.”

“Right. That’s true. But more importantly, it was to cut off their power.”

“Power, you mean...”

“No matter how much those people dislike each other, they have no choice but to unite when war breaks out. For example, don’t those who are plotting to kill each other in the palace unite when an enemy invades?”

“Yes.”

“If we had planted all kinds of seeds and conflicts and acted as if we were going to invade the Northern Lands, what would they have done?”

“They would have buried their grudges and tried to deal with us first.”

Jang Ilso nodded, pleased with the answer.

“Right. That would have been meaningless. That’s why I gave them enough time to fight each other.”

“...”

“It’s the same this time. If we back down now, all the responsibility will fall on Shaolin. That old man doesn’t want that to happen. That’s why he’s shouting over there right now.”

Jang Ilso spread his arms wide.

“War will break out soon! The moment this treaty ends, blood will start to flow and flow again! So now is not the time to blame anyone!”

“Certainly...”

“He is a treacherous old man.”

Jang Ilso shook his head.

“But... that treachery is also helpful to us. There’s nothing wrong with the clouds of war gathering. I will get the same benefits that Shaolin gets. In the meantime, I will steadily secure the pirates and suppress any complaints that might arise behind.”

Ho Ga-Myung stared across the river with a new look.

So strange.

As far as he knew, Jang Ilso and Shaolin had never communicated since Mount Hua withdrew from Plum Blossom Island.

Nevertheless, they were doing things that benefited both sides as if they had planned it. Jang Ilso was deploying the pirates ahead to give Shaolin something to say, and Shaolin was not leaving the riverside, buying time for Jang Ilso to fully take over the pirates.

‘Maybe this is the true collusion.’

With no need for conversation or documents, they were implicitly moving in a direction that benefited both sides.

Considering the meaning of the word collusion, could there be a situation more fitting than this?

“In the end, if you just buy time, everything will go as we planned.”

“...I hope so.”

However, unexpectedly, Jang Ilso stroked his chin with a slightly ambiguous expression.

“It was dangerous. It was close. If I had made one mistake, I could have lost my head.”

Although he wasn’t specifically referring to a certain place, Ho Ga-Myung couldn’t help but know where Jang Ilso was talking about.

“If there’s a variable, it would be Mount Hua. You never know what they will do. Especially that...”

Jang Ilso suddenly stopped talking.

He could somehow handle the one variable called Chung Myung. Just think of the worst-case scenario you can imagine. Chung Myung would surely follow that path.

But...

‘Hyun Jong.’

He couldn’t figure out where that old man was headed. He couldn’t understand his way of thinking at all.

‘That man is not sane.’

He could understand going after profit, and he could understand pursuing righteousness.

But how could someone who values his sect more than the law and at the same time demands sacrifices from the sect for others?

Where in the world can one find such an insane person?

“I don’t know. If the day comes when my neck falls off... it will be because of those damned bastards.”

“That will never happen.”

“Right. That must be it.”

A chilling killing intent flashed through Jang Ilso’s eyes as he stared across the river.

‘Not yet... no. Not yet.’

But that day would come.

The day when the entire Mount Hua would be stained with blood on his hands.

Jang Ilso’s tongue slowly brushed his red lips. It seemed as if the taste of blood lingered there.

The temporary residence that Shaolin and Kongtong hastily took along the Yangtze River was quiet, save for a cold voice coming from the innermost room.

“Explain it.”

The abbot sighed inwardly at the reprimand from Peng Yeop, the lord of the Peng family and wielder of the Lightning Swift Sword.

“Abbot.”

“I am listening, Lord Peng.”

“Is it really so difficult to answer why this happened?”

“Lord...”

Peng Yeop’s glare bore into the abbot as he spoke.

“I heard many stories on the way here, but one voice was the loudest: Shaolin abandoned the agreement and morality, forsaking Namgung on Plum Blossom Island.”

The abbot closed his eyes.

“And while Shaolin and Kongtong stood idle, the Heavenly Friends Alliance arrived first to rescue the Namgung family without suffering any casualties.”

“...”

“Abbot.”

Peng Yeop bit his lip and pressed on.

“Please answer me. Is everything I’ve heard true?”

“Amitabha.”

The abbot quietly chanted Buddhist prayers, as if trying to soothe his troubled mind. But to Peng Yeop, those prayers sounded like a man stepping back from responsibility.

“Say something!”

“Stop it!”

It wasn’t the abbot who raised his voice, but Jong Ri-Hyung, the Demon Subduing Saint and leader of the Kongtong sect. His eyes blazed with anger as he glared at Peng Yeop.

“Does Lord Peng have the right to say such things?”

“Why am I not qualified to speak?”

“While Shaolin and Kongtong were trying to act here along the Yangtze River, where was the Peng family?”

“...”

“Even though the Peng family is far from here, it’s not something for someone who just arrived to lecture us on how things should have been done! Especially when you didn’t even show up!”

“It took time to get permission from the family elders,” Peng Yeop replied.

“Is that even an excuse?”

Peng Yeop shot a menacing glare at Jong Ri-Hyung but had no retort; what was said was true. By the time they arrived, everything was already over.

“If only the Hubei Peng family had arrived on time, we could have devised a different plan! The reinforcements that were supposed to join us never came—what else could we do?”

“So you’re saying you just watched Namgung die?”

“Watch them die? Don’t be too harsh!”

“Was I wrong?”

Their voices grew louder and more heated.

The abbot silently observed their quarrel.

Peng Yeop, who looked ready to leap at Jong Ri-Hyung, suddenly sighed deeply.

“...I am sorry.”

“...”

Jong Ri-Hyung seemed speechless at Peng Yeop’s unexpected apology. Then, blushing, he coughed and said,

“No, I was too harsh, Lord Peng.”

“...I didn’t know I would arrive this late. Even the great elders opposed me.”

“Hmm.”

A family differed from a sect. Unlike a sect, which automatically accepts disciples when the time comes, a family is passed down through bloodlines, and succession only occurs when the next lord reaches a certain age.

The problem arises when succession happens too quickly.

Though they step down from the front lines, the great elders and former family lords still hold power and constantly interfere with the family lord’s every action, like a king.

The Namgung family avoided this because Namgung Hwang was the youngest, but this was a common occurrence. Didn’t the Sichuan Tang family face similar issues? It’s a chronic problem in family sects.

Facing a family-like sect, it’s difficult to cut down the great elders who meddle in the lord’s affairs.

“I understand.”

Jong Ri-Hyung nodded, though deep down he thought differently.

‘It’s fortunate the Peng family didn’t arrive on time.’

Would things have been different if they had?

Well, even if the Peng family had joined early, the abbot wouldn’t have changed his mind. That would have only invited greater criticism. Three sects that dominated the world had gathered, yet they merely watched the fire from across the river.

It was better to at least have the excuse that the Peng family arrived late, and they couldn’t intervene.

“So, what will you do now?”

“...What do you mean?”

“What’s done cannot be changed. Shouldn’t we discuss what comes next?”

Jong Ri-Hyung nodded at Peng Yeop’s words, already feeling frustrated. The abbot remained seated, silent.

“Abbot.”

Peng Yeop called, and finally, the abbot spoke.

“First...”

“Yes?”

“I must apologize. I feel ashamed that two sects have suffered unnecessary humiliation because of this old monk’s foolishness.”

The abbot bowed deeply.

“No, abbot.”

“Why do you say that?”

Jong Ri-Hyung tried to dissuade the abbot but smiled bitterly.

He was disgusted by the confrontation between the abbot and Chung Myung.

But that didn’t mean he could fight Shaolin. If Kongtong had another ally besides Shaolin, they wouldn’t have come all this way to the Yangtze River.

In any case, they were a sect on the same boat as Shaolin. They had no choice but to trust Shaolin, whether in life or death. The same was likely true for the Peng family.

The Namgung and Sichuan Tang families, the backbone of the Five Great Families, had left. That meant the remaining three families would soon enter a power struggle for supremacy.

Now, they should be called the Three Great Families instead of Five, but even that small prestige was a vital title in the martial arts world.

In that competition, the Peng family would definitely need Shaolin’s help. No matter how far Shaolin had fallen, it was still Shaolin.

Even two sects, not Namgung or Sichuan Tang, could be easily suppressed by Shaolin’s current strength.

This expedition was bold, undertaken despite opposition from the great elders and former family lords. To avoid criticism for returning empty-handed and with a bad reputation, Peng Yeop had to side with Shaolin.

In fact, Peng Yeop was in a position where he had to cling to the abbot more than Jong Ri-Hyung.

“But in the end, we can only know the outcome when everything concludes.”

“...Does that mean the situation along the Yangtze River isn’t over yet?”

“Yes.”

The abbot spoke seriously.

“Right now, everyone is fixated on something too small. The reason we called the Nine Great Sects wasn’t to save the Namgung family, right?”

“Ah...”

Jong Ri-Hyung looked at the abbot, shocked. He had forgotten this.

“We gathered because the Evil Four Precepts Alliance showed signs of invading the Northern Lands. It wasn’t to save the Namgung family.”

“Right, sect leader.”

Feeling a glimmer of hope, Jong Ri-Hyung nodded.

It might be a blind man’s bluff, but Jong Ri-Hyung knew some people believed in such bluffs.

“If we misunderstand our mission because of the world’s criticism, a greater disaster will come.”

“...Do you think the Four Precepts Alliance will attack the Northern Lands like this?”

“Yes. I think it’s highly likely.”

“But seriously...”

“If not, why would the pirate fleet still be in the Yangtze River? Besides, aren’t we bound by the Yangtze River treaty, preventing us from attacking the Southern Lands?”

“When you put it that way...”

Jong Ri-Hyung’s expression shifted subtly.

Logically, there was no reason for them to camp there.

Even if they were used to life on ships, would it be easy to wait indefinitely with poor supplies?

“Abbot, if they attack, it would be difficult for our three sects to handle them.”

To be honest, Jong Ri-Hyung didn’t fully understand the Evil Sect’s power. But he was certain of one thing: if war broke out now, the three sects would suffer greatly.

‘That must never happen.’

The abbot responded.

“Let’s send out a proclamation to the other sects.”

“A proclamation?”

“Yes.”

The abbot nodded.

“A final request for support sent in the name of Shaolin. This time, it will include the leaders of the Five Great Families, the Hubei Peng family, and the backbone of the Nine Great Sects, Kongtong. That will give it more weight.”

“Hmm.”

“I don’t know if they will respond, but at least we can properly inform them of the situation here.”

“Yes.”

Peng Yeop nodded.

Even if they didn’t come to the Yangtze River, these three sects could claim they were preventing the Four Sects Alliance’s invasion, so it was not a bad idea.

“But... isn’t the Heavenly Friends Alliance here too?”

The abbot shook his head at Jong Ri-Hyung’s question.

“Which is why we should send out a proclamation.”

“What do you mean?”

“I do not trust the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

“...Abbot.”

“I’m not denying their achievements. But no matter how I think about it, the fact that they entered Plum Blossom Island and returned without any casualties is simply suspicious.”

“...”

“Even if only twenty people had died, I wouldn’t say this. But did ten die? Or five? Only the Namgung family perished. Can we really trust the Heavenly Friends Alliance in such a situation?”

“That is certainly unnatural.”

Peng Yeop nodded.

Jong Ri-Hyung, who had witnessed the whole situation, still looked uneasy, but to Peng Yeop’s ears, the abbot’s words sounded convincing.

That meant to those who hadn’t seen the situation firsthand, the abbot’s words seemed close to the truth.

No, perhaps it was meaningless.

People preferred pleasant lies to uncomfortable truths, so Peng Yeop had to believe those words.

His conscience churned, but he suppressed it, recalling how the family elders looked at him when he left.

“So please, the two of you, help me. If you do, everything will fall into place.”

“Understood.”

“I will do that.”

After the conversation ended, a strange atmosphere filled the room. Unable to bear it, the two bowed their heads and rose from their seats. The abbot made no move to stop them.

Left alone, the abbot slowly held his prayer beads and rolled them through his fingers.

Right. It might be far-fetched. But...

‘If the Four Precepts Alliance really attacks the Northern Lands, will this still seem far-fetched?’

If that happens, the entire situation will change.

Those who criticized them would turn to criticize the Heavenly Friends Alliance. They saw that the alliance could have killed Jang Ilso but avoided sacrifice out of fear for their own lives. That is what brought about this whole situation.

Sometimes, instead of waiting for the tide to change, one must change it oneself.

‘If not me, then who?’

He closed his eyes tightly.

In the room where silence had fallen, only the faint sound of prayer beads rolling echoed.

“So...”

Hong Dae-Kwang swallowed hard. Of course, Chung Myung was sitting right in front of him now.

Why did it feel so obvious?

Tsk tsk. What kind of obvious talk was that! Just as a thread naturally followed the path of a needle, shouldn’t Hong Dae-Kwang be wherever Chung Myung went?

This time, Mount Hua had been a bit late joining them, but his job was to gather information from Mount Hua in the first place!

So now, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, who needed to assess the situation of the Kangho, had to wait anxiously for him.

Right. It should have been that way...

“Are you sure?”

“What are you asking”?

“Hmm. Can I really believe it...?”

“Oh my. Would I really be giving false information to the world’s Mount Hua? I am not a beggar with two lives.”

“Hmm.”

“Hehe. You can believe me.”

“Well, if you say so.”

“...”

Hong Dae-Kwang looked at the person sitting next to him with a trembling face.

Gentle Crow Beggar.

The elder of the Beggars Union... no, the former elder was sitting just a half step ahead of Hong Dae-Kwang, passing information to Mount Hua’s Sword Saint.

Information that even Hong Dae-Kwang didn’t know.

“A proclamation. He is using such a classic trick.”

Chung Myung muttered with a snicker.

“It isn’t like that’ll make any difference, but it’s the best they can do.”

“Yes. The letter is probably being sent to all the Nine Great Sects in the world right now.”

“It isn’t just flying then; it will probably be carried by the Beggars Union.”

“A-ahem.”

Gentle Crow Beggar coughed, redness rising on his face.

“The... Beggars Union, too, has a position.”

“Who said that?”

Chung Myung glared at him with narrowed eyes, and Gentle Crow Beggar averted his gaze.

“If you keep acting like a bat, your wings will get torn off.”

“Then why don’t we just crawl on our two legs? We can easily handle the fact that we are beggars rolling around on the ground.”

Hong Dae-Kwang stared blankly at Gentle Crow Beggar.

‘Yah, doesn’t this guy have any pride?’

Who was the Gentle Crow Beggar?

He unofficially acted as a proxy leader in the Beggars Union, where the leader was often absent due to illness.

To summarize, except for a sickbed, it was no exaggeration to say he was the current leader of the Beggars Union. Seeing Gentle Crow Beggar schmoozing up to Mount Hua’s Sword Saint, who was barely in his twenties, even Hong Dae-Kwang’s mouth fell open.

“Trust is a must.”

“Wouldn’t lost trust be something that can be recovered in the future?”

“Tsk.”

Chung Myung nodded while looking at Gentle Crow Beggar with a trembling face.

“Understood. If you have any other information, please let me know right away.”

“Of course we will. Isn’t that why I am here?”

“Such no-value words.”

Chung Myung waved his hand. Then Gentle Crow Beggar quickly nodded and got up from his seat.

“Then I will come to see you again.”

“Yes.”

“... and please speak to the Alliance leader too...”

“Ah, okay.”

“Yes, then.”

Gentle Crow Beggar, who had been bowing deeply as if he had no knees, suddenly turned around. Then, he kicked Hong Dae-Kwang in a completely different manner than before.

“What are you doing?”

“Uh?”

“Get out.”

“...”

Hong Dae-Kwang, dragged out in a daze, looked at Gentle Crow Beggar in confusion.

“N-No. Why is the elder here?”

“Why would he not be?”

“... N-no. That...”

A look of confusion crossed Hong Dae-Kwang’s face.

“Did the Beggars’ Union decide to completely tie their rope with Mount Hua?”

“Why would that happen?”

“Right. It cannot be...”

“The rope is supposed to be held with both hands.”

When Hong Dae-Kwang opened his mouth wide, Gentle Crow Beggar shrugged his shoulders.

“It seems that the Beggars Union leader doesn’t want to let go of the rope of the Heavenly Friends Alliance either.”

“Hehehe.”

Hong Dae-Kwang burst into laughter as if it were absurd.

Of course, it was no exaggeration to say this was something the Beggars’ Union had always done.

The Beggars’ Union had almost a monopoly on information from the Northern Lands and had secured a fair amount from the Southern lands, too. At least on the Justice faction side, the Beggars Union was irreplaceable.

So, unlike other sects, the Nine Great Sects was a faction that could moderately sway powerful groups without much trouble.

‘This is meaningful.’

Less repercussions did not mean no repercussions.

In other words, in the eyes of the Beggars Union, the Heavenly Friends Alliance had now become a force for which it could risk playing both sides.

So didn’t this mean that Hong Dae-Kwang’s choice to stake everything on Mount Hua was proven right, and his position has risen by leaps and bounds?

That would be good to think about... very good...

“No, but why are you dealing with that guy? Elder? That is what I want! Weren’t you supposed to leave matters related to Mount Hua to me?”

At that moment, Gentle Crow Beggar suddenly turned his head and glared at Hong Dae-Kwang with menacing eyes.

“Leave it to you?”

Hong Dae-Kwang flinched, overwhelmed by the force.

“Leave it to you? Right. You said it well. You beggar!”

“... Uh?”

“What on earth have you been doing all this time to make Mount Hua’s Sword Saint look at the Beggars Union with such disdain?”

“What kind of eyes? They are his eyes!”

“What on earth have you been doing all this time to make Mount Hua’s Sword Saint think of the Beggars Union as a bunch of idiots who do nothing but beg?”

Hong Dae-Kwang blinked.

“Isn’t that what we are?”

“What?”

“... Isn’t that what beggars are like? Begging without doing anything. Why would busy people even try to become beggars?”

“...”

“Why are you looking at me like that? Did I say something wrong?”

Gentle Crow Beggar looked up at the distant sky in silence.

‘What have I done?’

Why did he put this guy next to the Mount Hua sect? It was amazing how his ties with Mount Hua had remained intact until now.

“... Anyway, I will be managing this area myself for a while, just so you know.”

“Uh? Where is that kind of nonsense coming from? Even though you are an elder, you are just stepping into someone else’s work that has been ongoing for years!”

“Look at this incompetent bastard! Is this something to be concerned about?”

“A-anyway, I cannot acknowledge it! How much effort have I put into Mount Hua? Why have I endured such cruel persecution? Even if you are an elder, if you touch my territory, I will break your head!”

“Do you wish to die?”

“Do whatever you want! I am a beggar with nothing to lose anyway! If I die, I will just die!”

Looking at Hong Dae-Kwang, who was glaring, Gentle Crow Beggar grabbed his head.

“Ugh. This insane bastard...”

He was originally a guy with a weird side, but he wasn’t this insane. How did he end up being so incompetent?

‘Does the Hwa-Um water have anything in it?’

Even if it did, this man was turning dark. After being close to Mount Hua’s Sword Saint for several years, he had become like a young man without realizing it. Of course, if he were told about this face, Hong Dae-Kwang would have jumped up and down, saying it was ridiculous.

“Anyway, this is the Beggars Union leader’s orders, so don’t do anything!”

“Elder, you aren’t even part of the Beggars Union anymore!”

“So what? You want to get beaten up by someone who doesn’t even belong to the clan?”

“Ughhh.”

Hong Dae-Kwang groaned.

No matter how much he declared himself to be out of the Beggars Union, no one would think that Gentle Crow Beggar wasn’t part of it. Even if he got angry at someone who secretly returned when the time came, the only one who would suffer would be Hong Dae-Kwang.

‘Even so, it is true!’

Hong Dae-Kwang had been hanging on to Mount Hua since it was a third-rate sect no one paid attention to, and had suffered greatly. Who wouldn’t frown when their superior sneakily stepped into the place others have worked hard to build?

‘I won’t give it to you even if I die!’

Hong Dae-Kwang’s eyes turned red. Knowing his feelings, Gentle Crow Beggar gently coaxed him this time.

“Don’t worry. I am in charge of the part related to the Heavenly Friends Alliance. You can focus on Mount Hua like you always do.”

“... Are you sure?”

“This brat!”

“Ugh. If that’s true, then well.”

Gentle Crow Beggar shook his head. Actually, the Beggars Union leader seemed to want him to take charge of Mount Hua himself...

‘Then I won’t be able to die in peace.’

Gentle Crow Beggar was someone who would say whatever he wanted, even in front of the Shaolin abbot. However, the disciple named Chung Myung was difficult even for him to handle. Whenever he faced the young man, he felt like he was standing in front of a bomb that could explode at any moment.

‘So strange.’

He looked at the room where Chung Myung was with a strange gaze.

‘Of course, it could be burdensome because of the hasty temperament... but that isn’t everything.’

He felt a sense of intimidation greater than what he felt when dealing with the leader of the Beggars Union or the abbot of Shaolin. Considering Chung Myung’s experience and status in the Kangho, it was definitely not possible.

‘I think I understand why those who met Mount Hua’s Sword Saint had difficulty dealing with him.’

This was something that could not be expressed through words alone. That was why those who handled information should not blindly believe or be swayed by it.

The best way to confirm how great a force was to see who led it.

‘I think I understand why the Nine Great Sects are lost and the Heavenly Friends Alliance is rising.’

Hyun Jong and Chung Myung.

As long as these two led Mount Hua, the center of the Heavenly Friends Alliance, their force would grow stronger every day.

“But, is this alright?”

“Hmm? About what?”

Hong Dae-Kwang asked with a slightly concerned look.

“I mean, the Beggars Union leader decided to side with the Heavenly Friends Alliance.”

They surely provided information to Mount Hua and the Heavenly Friends Alliance. However, that was the only information entrusted to Hong Dae-Kwang. Sensitive information, such as internal facts and trends of the Nine Great Sects, was not relayed to him.

But the fact that Gentle Crow Beggar had decided to move meant the Beggars’ Union was providing sensitive information to the Heavenly Friends Alliance.

That meant...

“... Doesn’t that mean the Beggars Union leader has decided that the Heavenly Friends Alliance will be the force opposing the Nine Sects?”

“There’s no point in finding meaning in it. It already is that way.”

Gentle Crow Beggar shook his head. However, Hong Dae-Kwang couldn’t shake off his worries.

“... To be honest, I’m a bit worried. The Evil Sect is growing stronger day by day, threatening the Northern Lands, and the faction is splitting into two.”

“The world doesn’t always go as planned.”

“But...”

“That isn’t for us to worry about.”

“Uh?”

Gentle Crow Beggar made a slight gesture toward the room where Chung Myung was.

“Doesn’t that man know what we know?”

“...”

“Perhaps Mount Hua’s Sword Saint has been planning this situation for quite some time. So it is his job to discuss countermeasures.”

“Eh. He’s not as planned as you think, elder.”

“Well. What do you think?”

“I am right.”

Gentle Crow Beggar completed Hong Dae-Kwang’s words.

With this incident, the world was clearly divided into three.

South: Four Precepts Alliance; East: Nine Great Sects; and West: Heavenly Friends Alliance.

The boundaries were not yet set. However, the fact that the western part of the Central Plains had been swallowed by the Heavenly Friends Alliance was undeniable.

Gentle Crow Beggar didn’t believe in coincidence. In something like this, human will must be involved.

“It must be hard.”

“Uh?”

“N-no.”

When Hong Dae-Kwang asked, Gentle Crow Beggar shook his head.

But his gaze did not leave the pavilion where Chung Myung was.

Even though it may have taken several years to achieve, or the thought came after much reflection, it’s easy to guess how intense and complicated the mind of the person who achieved all this with his own hands must be.

“Just like a swan may look elegant when seen from above the water, but desperately flaps its legs underwater to stay afloat, Mount Hua’s Sword Saint is also secretly trying to maintain a frivolous appearance.”

“That’s a lie. Swans don’t flap their legs underwater; they simply float.”

“... For real?”

“...”

“...”

Gentle Crow Beggar coughed loudly.

“Anyway, he is an amazing person...”

Clack!

At that moment, the door of Chung Myung’s room swung open.

“Ah. Lately, the alcohol bottles have gotten smaller. I didn’t even take a couple of sips and they’re already empty...”

Chung Myung, walking with both hands full of pure white alcohol bottles, flinched when he saw the two people still standing in front of his room.

“...”

“...”

A strange silence filled the air for a moment. Chung Myung awkwardly held up one of the bottles.

“... Would you like some? There’s a little left.”

“...”

No, maybe it was just a mistake.

“That’s exactly what I thought!”

Namgung Do-Wei gripped Baek Cheon’s hand tightly.

Baek Cheon looked at him with trembling eyes.

“Thank you! Thank you, Taoist Baek Cheon!”

“N-No, wait a minute...”

Baek Cheon, feeling very uneasy, tried to quietly dissuade Namgung Do-Wei, but it seemed he was no longer willing to listen.

“I suspected it, and as expected! I thought Mount Hua’s incredible growth couldn’t be explained by existing techniques.”

“...”

“So it is true that Taoist Baek Cheon and all the members of Mount Hua are learning martial arts from Taoist Chung Myung?”

“... that’s not wrong... I mean, it isn’t wrong, but...”

“Thank you!”

Namgung Do-Wei released Baek Cheon’s hand and clenched his fist.

“It seems the path to revive the Namgung family has been decided now.”

“That... young lord? Please calm down first...”

“Then I will go ask Taoist Chung Myung! Thank you!”

“W-wait a minute...”

But Namgung Do-Wei ran off without even looking back. Baek Cheon, staring blankly after him, was met with criticism.

“Devil.”

“Pushing someone into the fire.”

“Ah, no, kids! What do you want me to do? He didn’t even give me a chance to explain, and only heard what he wanted!”

Baek Cheon grumbled in frustration, but the Five Swords had already turned their gaze away from him, looking pitifully at Namgung Do-Wei running ahead.

“... He will regret this.”

“Yes.”

“Poor guy.”

“That’s just fate, sago.”

The calmest among them was, of course, Yoon Jong.

“... Let’s follow him.”

“Huh?”

Yoon Jong said with a sense of duty on his face.

“If we can’t stop him from jumping into the fire, we should save him when the time is right and pour water on him.”

“...”

Though it seemed oddly out of place, Baek Cheon simply nodded.

“Right, we should try to stop him from burning.”

The Five Swords quickly followed Namgung Do-Wei.

“Ah, don’t push!”

“Wait, I need to see...”

“No, wait, I told you not to push!”

The Five Swords opened the door of the pavilion where Chung Myung was, clinging to the wall and peering inside.

A strange scene unfolded within.

Chung Myung sat like a powerful noble, while Namgung Do-Wei knelt before him.

At first glance, it looked like someone had come to ask for a favor after bribing him.

Like a corrupt official... no, Chung Myung, who looked a hundred times more sinister than any corrupt official, opened his mouth with a sullen expression as he looked at Namgung Do-Wei.

“So...”

“Yes! Taoist.”

“You want me to teach you how to use a sword?”

“Yes!”

Chung Myung smiled broadly.

“Not just you, but the entire Namgung family?”

“Yes!”

“Me?”

“Yes!”

“Haha!”

“Hahahaha!”

“Hahahahaha!”

Chung Myung and Namgung Do-Wei laughed loudly as if sharing a common purpose. Of course, only Namgung Do-Wei thought he understood the meaning.

“This bastard must have gone crazy!”

Puak!

“Acck!”

Namgung Do-Wei screamed as he flew backward after being kicked in the face by Chung Myung, who had jumped over the table.

“I knew this would happen!”

Baek Cheon burst through the door and quickly caught Namgung Do-Wei before he slammed into the wall.

“No!”

Chung Myung roared, stomping the floor.

“Who put drugs into the river without telling me!? There’s not a single sane brat left! The Green Forest bastard wants to join the Heavenly Friends Alliance! The Shaolin brat only cares about himself! And what about the brat who wants the Namgung family to learn swordsmanship after becoming the lord?”

“T-taoist, it’s not like that...”

“I’ve seen many bastards in my lifetime, but this one is on another level of stupidity! Yah! You insane bastard! Does any of that make sense?”

Baek Cheon, who had set down the gloomy Namgung Do-Wei, covered his hand with a fist and coughed softly.

“That... Chung Myung.”

“What!”

“I understand why you’re upset, but even the young lord is so anxious he’s acting like this. You have to understand...”

“Sasuk...”

“Uh?”

Chung Myung said with a serious expression.

“If we continue like this, we’ll all go down in flames.”

“Why do people always talk about going down in flames...”

“Anyway, we’re doomed. We’re going down in flames. The only ones left behind will be the kids.”

“... So, what’s wrong?”

“But if Jo Gul sahyung, who survived, goes to Wudang with the others left behind and begs them to teach fighting, how would the sasuk who passed away feel about it?”

“This insane bastard!”

“Kuak!”

Baek Cheon kicked Jo Gul, who was standing there clueless. Jo Gul doubled over in pain and fell.

“Ah, I was a step too late...”

Yoon Jong licked his lips and lowered his raised hand. This time, Baek Cheon was quicker.

“Noooo!”

Jo Gul, rolling on the floor, raised his head with wide eyes.

“Why are you hitting me? Why! I didn’t really do it! So why?

“We’re doing this because we think you would actually do it.”

“...”

Jo Gul, speechless, turned to Yoon Jong, who simply nodded with a faint smile.

Betrayed by the world, Jo Gul crouched down, wrapping his arms around his knees. Tang Soso, nearby, patted him on the shoulder, feeling sorry for him.

“Right, you should have acted like a human from the start.”

“Kuak! Will you not leave right now?”

Baek Cheon coughed in vain and glanced at Namgung Do-Wei.

‘This isn’t right.’

He hadn’t thought much of it before, but changing his perspective, he realized there was no one like that insane guy. Of course, that was the truth. Where else would one learn sword technique?

Even Hyun Jong, famous for not showing pride in Mount Hua, didn’t ask the Southern Edge sect to teach swordsmanship, even though Mount Hua was in a terrible situation then.

No, if a disciple had suggested such a thing, even Hyun Jong would have stuck a plum branch in their face.

Chung Myung shouted, enraged.

“Yah, you insane bastard. Where did you learn the sword? Namgung Hwang in the tomb will rise up, coughing out Yangtze river water!”

“... My father isn’t buried yet.”

“Then go confirm it, brat! He might be trying to break through the coffin right now!”

Namgung Do-Wei flinched at Chung Myung’s force.

Chung Myung clicked his tongue and shook his head in disbelief.

“The world is about to fall. It must be falling now. What the hell happened in the past 100 years that the world is now filled with idiots like these?”

“Now these brats are scarier than the Heavenly Demon. Scarier than the Heavenly Demon, you morons!”

But Namgung Do-Wei protested as if it were unfair.

“I-I am not asking you to teach me Mount Hua’s sword techniques, Taoist.”

“Uh?”

“No matter how insane I may be, how could I ask you to teach me Mount Hua’s sword technique?”

“Right?”

Chung Myung, suddenly calm, nodded with a face that said, ‘Well, that’s right.’

“That’s good, then. For the future of the Namgung family, I was thinking whether I should throw you into the Yangtze River and make Namgung Myung the head of the family.”

“...”

“Then what?”

“Of course, I will learn the Namgung family’s sword technique. Namgung isn’t weak enough to covet other sects’ techniques!”

“It’s a little weak, though.”

“...”

“Uh... no, well. Anyway, continue.”

“... So, the thing is...”

Namgung Do-Wei, feeling somewhat sad, wiped his eyes and continued.

“What we want from you, Taoist, is not sword technique teachings, but experience in real combat.”

“Uh?”

“I felt it deeply during Plum Blossom Island. Learning sword technique without error, operating it perfectly, and identifying weaknesses through sparring with each other...”

Namgung Do-Wei’s attitude gradually became serious.

“That alone is not enough for real combat.”

“...”

Chung Myung looked at Namgung Do-Wei with interest.

‘He’s an interesting guy.’

In this incident, he lost two-thirds of the family sect’s power and even the lordship of the family. Because of that, the time for him to inherit the lord position came at least 20 years earlier.

An ordinary person would be out of their mind in such a situation. However, Namgung Do-Wei was already looking ahead.

“It might not be something to say in such a situation, but the Namgung family’s sword is by no means dull.”

Chung Myung nodded slightly. It wasn’t wrong. If the Namgung family had been weak, they wouldn’t have maintained their position as the best family sect in the world for so long.

“Even so, the reason the Namgung family is in such a great crisis this time is because our sword was like a sword in a well, unaware of how to fight in the real world.”

Namgung Do-Wei stared straight at Chung Myung.

“However, Mount Hua was different. The Mount Hua sect members probably didn’t have much fighting experience, but they fought as if they’d been through countless battles.”

Namgung Do-Wei clenched his fists.

“Taoist.”

“...”

“Please help us. We absolutely must become stronger now. And I think the only person who can do that job is a Taoist.”

“Hmm.”

Chung Myung scratched his cheek with an odd look, then spoke to Namgung Do-Wei in a slightly different tone than before.

“Well, everything is right, but... let me ask you one thing.”

“Yes.”

“I understand why, but I think that’s something Namgung could do without asking to learn it from Mount Hua.”

“...”

“I’m saying this because I don’t think you don’t know how dangerous that is. People in the world won’t consider such circumstances. They’ll just say the Namgung family is insane for learning swordsmanship from Mount Hua.”

“... I know.”

“Yeah, you know. I think you know. That’s why I’m asking.”

Chung Myung said coldly.

“If you know all that, why are you doing this?”

“...”

Namgung Do-Wei bit his lips tightly.

“... No more...”

“Hmm?”

“Because I don’t want to lose anyone anymore.”

Chung Myung’s face hardened at those words.

“The existence of the Four Precepts Alliance will make the world more and more dangerous. Unless you choose to close the Gate... no, even if you do, the Namgung family can no longer avoid them.”

The Five Swords listened and nodded. There was certainly truth in those words.

“Father told me.”

“... Namgung Hwang? The lord?”

“Yes. Father showed me at the last moment what the lord of the Namgung family should do.”

Namgung Do-Wei said with firm eyes.

“The lord of the Namgung family is the one who protects the Namgung family.”

“...”

“I... I don’t want to lose a single family member anymore. I will do anything for that.”

Namgung Do-Wei raised his head and looked at Chung Myung.

“If you tell me to kneel, I will kneel. If you tell me to bow, I will bow.”

Then he bowed deeply and shouted.

“Please help me, Taoist. That debt will be repaid by the Namgung sword!”

Baek Cheon, who had been watching, nodded.

‘That’s amazing.’

It was enough to make him feel ashamed.

‘If it were me, would I be able to bow my head to someone else like that for the sake of Mount Hua’s future?’

Namgung Do-Wei lived in a much higher status than Baek Cheon. Wasn’t the position of eldest son of the Namgung family one everyone in the murim world aspired to?

But Namgung Do-Wei’s actions now showed no hesitation. Regardless of right or wrong, that attitude was definitely worth emulating.

“I won’t lose a single person...”

Chung Myung snickered as he briefly recalled Namgung Do-Wei’s words.

“You can say such dreamy words even after seeing hell?”

“... I think I should say it even more because I saw it.”

“Your mouth is still alive, brat.”

A smile formed at the corners of Chung Myung’s mouth.

However, Baek Cheon knew the smile wasn’t meant badly. He sometimes smiled like that when they did more than Chung Myung expected.

“Well, fine. Even if it’s a real fight, it’s not that hard since you just have to beat the people up.”

“T-then?”

“We’re already an alliance. I will help you.”

“T-taoist!”

Namgung Do-Wei looked at Chung Myung with a moved face and shining eyes.

“Thank you...”

He was about to bow deeply again, but flinched and stopped. Chung Myung’s palm was waiting where he was about to bow.

“T-taoist.”

Namgung Do-Wei raised his head, puzzled.

And he saw a delighted Chung Myung, literally overjoyed.

After waiting and fighting for so long, when Jiang Taigong finally caught a moonfish, this must be the face he had.

“So.”

“...”

“How much are you going to pay?”

“... Uh?”

Namgung Do-Wei’s eyes widened.

“You said you don’t want to lose even one person.”

“...”

“If you say it the other way around, that means each and every life is that expensive, right?”

“... Taoist?”

“Then let’s find out.”

Chung Myung let out a wicked laugh.

“How much is the Namgung family willing to pay to save those people?”

“...”

“Don’t disappoint me. The Namgung family is rich, right?”

“...”

“Ehehehehe! Ehehehehehe!”

As Chung Myung laughed, a pure white marten suddenly jumped from his arms and climbed onto his head, mimicking the same pose and laughter.

Only after seeing this bizarre sight did Namgung Do-Wei realize something was seriously wrong.

“Why is the list of assets suddenly...?”

“...”

“Ah, of course. Since the young lord is now the head of the family, you have the right to inspect the family’s assets. But why now...?”

Namgung Myung looked at Namgung Do-Wei with suspicion. Namgung Do-Wei mumbled with a hollow expression.

“Just...”

“Uh?”

“Don’t ask... just answer. Just...”

“...”

What on earth had happened to the man who left the house this morning full of confidence, only to return with eyes like someone who had lost everything gambling?

Namgung Myung, who had been eyeing Namgung Do-Wei warily, said nervously,

“Then I will hand you the organized ledger.”

“Uncle.”

“...Why do you keep doing that?”

“Our Namgung family is surely rich, right?”

“Hehe.”

Namgung Myung laughed as if it were absurd.

“What are you talking about? They say there are many merchants in the world, and many people so wealthy they don’t need to work. But no matter how great the wealth, it can’t compare to the Namgung family’s fortune.”

His statement was full of pride.

Of course, as far as Namgung Myung knew, the only sect that could rival the Namgung family’s wealth was Shaolin. Even Shaolin or the Peng family were, in reality, inferior to the Namgung family.

However, upon hearing this, Namgung Do-Wei didn’t look pleased; instead, he began biting his nails nervously.

“We are rich... we are rich... rich, huh.”

“Y-young lord?”

“So we... are rich...”

Was this guy insane?

Clench.

Namgung Do-Wei, who had been biting his fingernails, finally opened his trembling lips.

“Where can I get the ledger?”

“If it’s a copy, the finance leader should have it.”

“...Right.”

Watching Namgung Do-Wei walk away, Namgung Myung was overcome with an inexplicable sense of anxiety.

He could vaguely hear Namgung Do-Wei mumbling, “We’re doing well, we’re doing well. We have to do well, please let us do well.”

“What the hell...”

Namgung Myung still didn’t know the exact nature of the disaster approaching the Namgung family.

Chung Myung’s eyes grew as wide as lanterns.

His mouth fell open involuntarily, and his hands holding the ledger trembled as if chilled.

“...T-this is...”

“...”

“Is this all?”

Namgung Do-Wei nodded his head, trembling.

“This is the list of assets of the Namgung family.”

“...O-oh my god.”

Chung Myung’s head creaked as he turned on its own. Even if the Heavenly Demon appeared before him, he wouldn’t have been so shocked.

He kept looking back and forth between Namgung Do-Wei and the ledger, speaking his thoughts without holding back.

“I wondered why people starve despite working hard, but you guys had all the money. You thieves.”

“...”

“Uh... what is this... hehehe.”

Chung Myung looked at the ledger again with a face that said he was fed up.

To be clear, Mount Hua was no longer a sect without money. It might not have been so a few years ago, but now it could be called one of the richest sects in the world.

Their accumulated wealth might not rival the richest in the Central Plains, but considering their income, they could now outmatch most sects.

In terms of income alone, they could boast of surpassing Mount Hua’s past poverty. But...

“This... damn. They say those who really have money have more than we.”

Baek Cheon slowly approached, curious about Chung Myung’s intense reaction.

“How much money do they even have?”

“See.”

Chung Myung handed the ledger to Baek Cheon without explanation.

Baek Cheon flipped through a few pages and casually said,

“No, it’s possible to have lots of money. It’s the Namgung family’s world. But it’s a bit much to say to the young lord...”

At that moment, Yoon Jong witnessed a sight he had never seen before.

Baek Cheon’s eyes slowly widened until they nearly popped.

“T-this...”

Flutter flutter!

Baek Cheon’s hands, turning over the ledger, began moving violently.

He had been roughly flipping through the pages with an expression that didn’t match his neat face, but suddenly stopped. Then he raised his head and blankly looked at Namgung Do-Wei.

But that was only for a moment, as his blank expression quickly shifted into one of resentment. Namgung Do-Wei mumbled.

“W-what is it?”

“You rich brat...”

“Uh?”

Namgung Do-Wei was so shocked he doubted his ears. Did those words really come from Baek Cheon?

At that moment, Chung Myung shouted,

“You have so much money, why bother wielding a sword, you insane bastard!”

“W-what is that even supposed to mean...”

“The rich get richer! They’re really getting richer!”

A voice full of resentment flowed from Chung Myung’s mouth.

“Damn it. There’s a limit to how unfair the world can be! Some people are born orphans and barely survive in Mount Hua, which is worse than a beggar’s den! Some are born as sons of the Namgung family!”

“I know!”

“Sasuk is from Southern Edge!”

“Compared to Namgung, Southern Edge is also a beggar’s den!”

“Ah, that’s right.”

“Filthy rich bastards.”

“Damned landowners.”

Jo Gul rubbed his eyes as he watched the scene. Somehow, it seemed like a red aura was surging behind Chung Myung and Baek Cheon. He thought he could see a sickle and hammer...

“Oh my... I thought I had a lot of money until now.”

Chung Myung mumbled with a blank expression.

Now he could see clearly. That damned Chung Mun sahyung’s brainwashing was narrowing his world. No matter how much Chung Myung boasted about making a lot of money, in the end, he was just a Taoist fool, happy with food and drink.

“Wait. So the Shaolin and Wudang brats have about the same amount of money, then?”

Chung Myung clenched his teeth.

“But these brats still want funds to raise their sects? I should chase these guys and dig up their bloody graves, too.”

“...What are you talking about? Since when?”

“Kuak.”

Chung Myung scratched his neck as if he had swallowed hot charcoal.

Yu Yiseol, curious about their foaming-at-the-mouth reaction, quietly approached Chung Myung and snatched the ledger.

Then.

Thud.

“Kuak! Sago! Why are you collapsing?”

Tang Soso ran to Yu Yiseol, who had fallen back in shock.

Chung Myung clicked his tongue as he watched.

“Right, right. It’s normal for someone from the streets to faint when they see this much money.”

“Sago is no orphan!”

“Something similar.”

“No, how much money does he have to make such a mess of us?”

Tang Soso picked up the ledger and looked at it.

“Hmm.”
